# arogya — Decision Log

Append-only. One entry per significant decision. Never edit past entries — if a decision is reversed, add a new entry explaining why.

Format:
```
---
**Date:** YYYY-MM-DD
**Decision:** one-line summary
**Context:** why this came up
**Choice:** what was decided
**Alternatives considered:** what else was on the table
**Reasoning:** why this choice was made
```

---

**Date:** 2026-05-11
**Decision:** Use `docs/progress.md` + `docs/decisions.md` for session continuity rather than inline CLAUDE.md state
**Context:** Sessions will be heavy/context-loaded; need a way for fresh sessions to pick up exactly where previous left off without manual copy-paste
**Choice:** Two separate docs injected via `SessionStart` hook — `progress.md` for operational state (auto-injected), `decisions.md` as append-only archive (read on demand)
**Alternatives considered:** Single combined doc; inline state in CLAUDE.md; no structured approach
**Reasoning:** Separating operational state (changes frequently) from decision archive (append-only) keeps both docs useful. Auto-injection via `SessionStart` hook means zero manual effort for context continuity.

**Date:** 2026-05-11
**Decision:** Scaffold Phase A.1 with Next.js 16 + React 19 + Tailwind v4 + Drizzle (postgres-js) + Zod 4
**Context:** Phase A item 1 — project init per design doc Phase 9.1 and 10.2
**Choice:** `create-next-app@16.2.6` (TS strict, App Router, ESLint, Tailwind, no src/, `@/*` alias, `--disable-git`); Drizzle ORM with the `postgres` driver; Zod 4 for env validation; `lib/env.ts` fails loudly on missing required vars
**Alternatives considered:** Pin to Next 14 LTS (rejected — design doc says "14+"; landing on latest is fine and avoids upgrade work); `pg` driver instead of `postgres` (rejected — Drizzle docs prefer `postgres` for serverless); install all v1 deps upfront (rejected — defer per phase boundary, smaller surface for v1.5 version pinning)
**Reasoning:** Latest framework versions keep us aligned with current Next.js docs. Drizzle + `postgres` is the design-doc-recommended pair. Zod 4 has API tweaks vs. v3 but `safeParse`/`flatten` are unchanged; URL/min validators still apply. Tailwind v4 ships CSS-first config (no `tailwind.config.ts`) — this matches Phase 7's design-token-via-CSS direction. Deferred to later phases: shadcn/ui init + palette (Phase C, first component); `@supabase/supabase-js` (Phase A item 7); `@anthropic-ai/sdk` + `ai` (Phase B); `react-hook-form`, `date-fns` (Phase C); CI workflows (before first PR to main). Kept the auto-generated `AGENTS.md` (single-line Next 16 warning) — it complements CLAUDE.md without competing as source of truth.

**Date:** 2026-05-12
**Decision:** Use Supabase **Session pooler** URL for `DATABASE_URL` (not Direct connection or Transaction pooler) in v1 dev
**Context:** During Phase A.1 verification, the Direct connection host (`db.<project-ref>.supabase.co`) failed DNS lookup. Supabase has retired Direct connection on new projects; only the pooler endpoints resolve.
**Choice:** Session pooler URL — host `aws-0-<region>.pooler.supabase.com:5432`, username `postgres.<project-ref>` (note the dot). Same single URL serves Drizzle Kit migrations AND Next.js dev runtime.
**Alternatives considered:** Transaction pooler (port 6543) — rejected for v1 dev because it does not support prepared statements, which breaks Drizzle migrations. Maintaining two `DATABASE_URL`s (session for migrations, transaction for runtime) — rejected as premature; revisit when Vercel serverless deploy lands (v1.5 or production).
**Reasoning:** Session pooler is the only endpoint that (a) actually resolves and (b) supports both prepared statements (needed by `drizzle-kit migrate`) and ad-hoc queries (needed by app code). The transaction-pooler split becomes relevant only when running on Vercel Functions where connections need to be short-lived; v1 demo runs locally and on Vercel with sufficient pool capacity from the session endpoint. Worth re-evaluating before production deploy.

**Date:** 2026-05-12
**Decision:** Add `tsx` as dev dep; create `scripts/check-env.ts` + `scripts/check-db.ts` as repo utilities
**Context:** Needed a way to run TypeScript files (e.g. `lib/env.ts`) standalone to verify env validation and DB connectivity. Forward-compatible with design doc 9.5's `scripts/backup.ts` and Phase A item 5's seed script.
**Choice:** Install `tsx` (esbuild-based TS runner, no config required). Add two smoke-test scripts: `check-env.ts` (imports `lib/env`, exits if Zod validation fails) and `check-db.ts` (opens a `postgres` connection, runs `select 1`, reports server version). Both runnable via `npx tsx --env-file=.env.local scripts/<name>.ts`.
**Alternatives considered:** `ts-node` (older, slower, more config); compile to JS via `tsc` before running (verbose); skip helpers and rely on `next dev` smoke test (less direct, doesn't actually exercise env-importing code).
**Reasoning:** `tsx` is the modern default for TS scripts; near-zero cost to install and serves multiple later use cases. The two helpers are tiny (<20 lines each) and become load-bearing the next time env/DB config changes (e.g. swapping to Transaction pooler for production). Keeping them in-repo means smoke tests are reproducible across sessions and contributors.

**Date:** 2026-05-12
**Decision:** Lock Phase A item 2 schema with pgEnum for all enums, ABO/Rh values for `blood_type`, and `restrict` (not cascade) for required cross-entity FKs in clinical history
**Context:** Phase A item 2 (schema) had three tactical decisions not prescribed by design.md Phase 4 / 9.2: (1) how to implement enum columns, (2) what values to use for `blood_type` (Phase 4 says "enum" but doesn't enumerate), (3) `onDelete` policy on required FKs other than `patient_id` (where Phase 9.2 mandates cascade).
**Choice:** (1) Native Postgres `pgEnum` — true ENUM types, one declaration per enum, snake_case names. (2) `blood_type` values: standard ABO/Rh set (`A+ | A- | B+ | B- | AB+ | AB- | O+ | O-`). (3) Required cross-entity FKs in clinical records default to `onDelete: "restrict"`. In v1 only `visits.doctor_id` qualifies; pattern applies forward. Cascade reserved for `patient_id` (Phase 9.2 mandate) and parent-child pairs where the child has no independent meaning (change logs, `lab_results→lab_reports`, `symptom_episodes→symptom_types`, `extraction_sessions→reports`).
**Alternatives considered:** (1) `text` + CHECK constraint — easier to add/remove values via simple ALTER TABLE, but no automatic Drizzle TS union, more boilerplate. Rejected because pgEnum is cleaner and the cost of adding values later (`ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE`) is acceptable. (2) Inline ABO/Rh values vs. broader set with "unknown" — went with the standard 8 since Phase 4 isn't prescriptive and ABO/Rh is the universal clinical set. (3) `cascade` on `visits.doctor_id` — rejected during /check review: a doctor row deletion would silently nuke a patient's entire visit history. UI should guide merge/reassign instead.
**Reasoning:** pgEnum aligns with Drizzle's recommended pattern for typed enums and gives free TS unions via `$inferSelect`. The ABO/Rh choice will appear in the patient form's blood-type dropdown — call out at UI build time if the patient population needs a broader set (e.g. include "unknown" or "Rh null"). The restrict-by-default FK policy is now a project-wide rule saved to memory (`feedback-fk-delete-behavior`); apply forward to any new required cross-entity FK.

**Date:** 2026-05-12
**Decision:** Include Phase 9.4 schema additions (extraction_sessions table, nullable `source_report_id` FK on 7 derivable entities, `reports.status` enum) in the Phase A item 2 migration
**Context:** Phase A item 2 per CLAUDE.md says "all 14 tables per Phase 4 + 9.2". But design.md Phase 10.2 line 2959 explicitly lists `extraction_sessions` as part of item 2, and line 3030 says "Schema with all 14 tables before any agent code". Phase 9.4 also mandates a nullable `source_report_id` FK on Medication, Condition, LabReport, VitalReading, SymptomEpisode, Visit, JournalEntry, plus a `reports.status` enum (`extracting | ready | failed | committed`). Question: do these ship now or during Phase E?
**Choice:** Ship now. Migration `0000_initial_schema.sql` includes the extraction_sessions table, all 7 `source_report_id` columns (nullable, FK→reports, ON DELETE SET NULL), and the `reports.status` enum with default `ready`.
**Alternatives considered:** Defer to Phase E and ship a follow-up migration when the file pipeline lands. Rejected because (a) Phase 10.2 explicitly puts this in item 2, (b) schema lockdown is in Phase A so adding columns later still requires a migration anyway, (c) zero runtime cost — nullable cols sit unused until extraction is wired up.
**Reasoning:** Schema is locked once `0000_initial_schema.sql` is applied to dev — every subsequent change is a new migration. Cleaner to land the full schema in one initial migration than to ship two. The `reports.status` default of `ready` ensures rows entered manually (i.e. not via extraction) start in the correct terminal state without requiring callers to set status. The `source_report_id` columns enable the extraction traceability requirement in 9.4 ("extraction source link persists on entity pages after commit") without needing schema changes during Phase E.

**Date:** 2026-05-12
**Decision:** Keep both `patients.family_history` (markdown column) AND the structured `family_history` table
**Context:** Phase 4 has both a free-text `family_history` column on patients (design.md:244) AND a structured `family_history` table (design.md:541). The column's note literally says "Promotes to a structured table in v2 if patterns emerge" — but the structured table is already in v1. The two surfaces overlap.
**Choice:** Keep both. The `patients.family_history` column holds narrative / cross-cutting prose ("paternal MI history at 65, maternal diabetes onset 50, etc."); the `family_history` table holds individual entries the AI can query per-relation. Doc note at design.md:244 is stale.
**Alternatives considered:** Drop the column and rely on the structured table's per-entry `notes` field for prose context. Rejected because the user wants a singleton narrative surface in addition to per-relation rows — useful for entries that don't fit any single relation cleanly.
**Reasoning:** Two surfaces, no overlap if used carefully: column = patient-level narrative summary, table = per-relation rows. Vault context serializer will need to include both when building synthesis context — flag for Phase B work. design.md:244 should be amended during a future doc cleanup pass.

---

**Date:** 2026-05-12
**Decision:** `updated_at` columns auto-update at the Drizzle ORM level (`$onUpdate(() => new Date())`), not via a database trigger
**Context:** Every table in the schema has `created_at` and `updated_at` columns per Phase 4 convention (line 219: "assume present on every table"). Postgres does not auto-update timestamps on UPDATE — the mechanism has to come from somewhere.
**Choice:** Drizzle's `.$onUpdate(() => new Date())` modifier. All `updated_at` columns get this treatment in every schema file.
**Alternatives considered:** Database trigger (PL/pgSQL function set in the migration). Rejected: more migration noise, harder to reason about test fixtures, and v1 has no use case where rows are updated outside Drizzle (no direct psql writes, no RLS, no other ORM).
**Reasoning:** App-level auto-update keeps the schema migration clean and matches Drizzle docs. Trade-off (worth knowing): direct SQL writes via psql, Drizzle Studio raw queries, or any future non-Drizzle client will NOT touch `updated_at`. Revisit in v1.5 if real auth + RLS + direct-from-Supabase reads land — at that point a trigger is the safer bet.

**Date:** 2026-05-12
**Decision:** Stub auth IDs are v4 UUID literals exported as shared constants from `lib/auth.ts`; seed imports them; return-object field names are `userId` / `patientId` (matching design doc 9.6:2737-2753 verbatim, not the ergonomic `id`)
**Context:** Phase A items 4 + 5. Design doc 9.6:2737-2753 shows stub return values like `userId: "user_demo_avi"`, `patientId: "patient_demo_ramesh"` — but `patients.id` is a `uuid` column in the locked schema, so the doc's snake_case string literals cannot satisfy the FK constraint. Separately, the seed and auth helpers both need the SAME fixed IDs forever, with no drift risk.
**Choice:** Generated two v4 UUIDs once, hardcoded as `STUB_USER_ID` / `STUB_PATIENT_ID` exports from `lib/auth.ts`. `db/seed.ts` imports both constants and uses them for the Ramesh INSERT (`patients.id = STUB_PATIENT_ID`, `patients.owner_user_id = STUB_USER_ID`). Return-object field names match the doc spec exactly: `getCurrentUser()` → `{ userId, name, email }`; `getCurrentPatient()` → `{ patientId, name }`. Email value `avi@arogya.local` (`.local` TLD signals stub data).
**Alternatives considered:** (a) doc's snake_case string IDs — rejected (schema requires uuid). (b) duplicate UUID literals in both `lib/auth.ts` and `db/seed.ts` — rejected (drift risk). (c) `id` field names on return objects — initially shipped; /check caught the deviation against 9.6:2737-2753 and we renamed. (d) Deterministic UUIDs via `uuidv5` — over-clever; the values aren't meaningful, only stable.
**Reasoning:** Co-locating the constants with the auth helpers is the natural single-source-of-truth pattern — rename or rotate in one place. Matching the doc's field names exactly (even though `id` would be ergonomically cleaner at call sites like `db.select().where(eq(patients.id, patient.id))`) keeps call sites aligned with the spec, so the v1.5 swap to real auth replaces only the helper bodies. Future call sites MUST read `user.userId` and `patient.patientId`, never `.id`.

**Date:** 2026-05-12
**Decision:** `lib/storage.ts` uses `import "server-only"`; `scripts/setup-storage.ts` therefore cannot import `STORAGE_BUCKET` and inlines `"arogya"` with a cross-reference comment
**Context:** Phase A item 7. `lib/storage.ts` uses `supabaseAdmin` (service-role key) — must never bundle into client code. The bucket-creation script needs the same bucket name. Next 16 does not ship `server-only` transitively, so it was installed as a dep.
**Choice:** `import "server-only"` at the top of `lib/storage.ts` as a build-time guard against client-bundle inclusion. `scripts/setup-storage.ts` runs via tsx (not in a Next bundle) — `server-only` throws at runtime in that context, so the script cannot import anything from `lib/storage.ts`. Setup script declares `const BUCKET = "arogya"` locally with a comment cross-referencing `STORAGE_BUCKET` in `lib/storage.ts`. Path builders dropped from `lib/storage.ts` for now (Phase 9.4:2612 only names the three I/O helpers; first caller is Phase E `/api/files/sign`).
**Alternatives considered:** (a) hoist bucket name into its own file (e.g. `lib/storage-bucket.ts`) that both `lib/storage.ts` and the setup script import — rejected as over-modular for one string. (b) drop the `server-only` guard — rejected; credential-protection guarantee is worth a tiny duplication. (c) include path builders (`storagePaths.upload(patientId, filename)` etc.) — rejected per CLAUDE.md "don't pre-build"; design doc enumerates path patterns but its helpers list (line 2612) does not include path builders, and committing to a shape before the first caller exists locks in drift.
**Reasoning:** `server-only`'s runtime-throw behavior is intentional — respecting it means accepting one string duplication, made explicit and grep-able via the cross-reference comment. Path builders get added when `/api/files/sign` lands in Phase E and the exact upload-flow shape is known. Bucket-level RLS deferred to v1.5 per design doc 9.4:2546.

**Date:** 2026-05-12
**Decision:** Phase A query helpers ship a minimal first cut — `forPatient(patientId)` per entity plus `active(patientId)` only for medications + conditions; exported as per-entity namespace objects; change-log and specialized helpers deferred until a UI surface needs them
**Context:** Phase A item 6. Phase 9.2:2313-2322 prescribes `db/queries/` with per-entity helpers taking `patientId` as an explicit param (RLS-ready); gives `medications.ts (forPatient, active, ...)` as the only concrete example. Open questions: how broad should the first cut be (the indexing strategy at 9.2:2297-2306 implies several access patterns), and how should the helpers be exported?
**Choice:** 14 files in `db/queries/` mirroring `db/schema/` — singular file naming, matching the existing schema convention (design doc 9.2:2316-2319 example uses plural; doc is internally inconsistent with its own singular schema list). Each file exports a typed namespace object (`patientQueries`, `medicationQueries`, …) so the barrel `db/queries/index.ts` is collision-free even when multiple entities have a same-named helper (`active()` on both medications and conditions). Coverage shipped: `forPatient(patientId)` on every entity (or `getById` for the patient root, `getForPatient` for the lifestyle singleton, `forReport`/`forPatient` paired for lab + symptom child entities), plus `active(patientId)` for medications and conditions (the two cases the design doc explicitly cites). Helpers are async, return resolved row arrays from `db.select().from(...).where(eq(..., patientId))` SQL-style queries.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) build the full set the indexing strategy implies (recent-by-type for vitals, date-windowed for episodes, change-log `forParent(parentId)`, child-by-parent for `lab_results.lab_report_id`) — rejected per CLAUDE.md "don't pre-build for hypothetical requirements"; add when the first caller lands. (b) flat per-file function exports (`listMedicationsForPatient`) — rejected, less ergonomic at call sites and risks barrel collisions. (c) Drizzle relational query API (`db.query.medications.findMany(...)`) — rejected; SQL-style is more explicit about which columns and joins are involved, and matches most Drizzle examples in circulation.
**Reasoning:** Query helpers exist to compress the common patient-scoped read at call sites AND to be the seam for v1.5 RLS — taking `patientId` as an explicit parameter today means flipping on RLS later doesn't change any signatures. Building only what callers actually need (and what the design doc cites by name) keeps the surface honest. The namespace-object export style gives autocomplete and a stable barrel as new helpers get added. Change-log and specialized helpers get added when their UI surface lands in Phase D (medication/condition detail pages).

**Date:** 2026-05-13
**Decision:** Phase B will use a two-SDK split for agent code — `ai` + `@ai-sdk/anthropic` for streaming agents (synthesis, onboarding) and raw `@anthropic-ai/sdk` for non-streaming agents (extraction, router, insight, auto-titling)
**Context:** Design doc 9.3:2384-2406 prescribes both SDKs in its example snippets — `streamText` from `ai` for streaming surfaces and direct `anthropic.messages.create` from the raw SDK for non-streaming agents. CLAUDE.md only listed `@anthropic-ai/sdk` as a Phase B dependency. Needed an explicit call before SDKs get installed at the start of B5.
**Choice:** Install both. Streaming surfaces use the Vercel AI SDK's `streamText` + `useChat` ergonomics (one-line model swap, built-in stream protocol). Non-streaming agents use the raw SDK directly for full control over timeouts, retries, error handling, and JSON output parsing.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) Use only `@anthropic-ai/sdk` and roll our own stream handling for synthesis — rejected; reinventing what `streamText` solves is wasted effort and the AI SDK's `useChat` pairs cleanly with the Phase C chat surface. (b) Use only the AI SDK for everything — rejected; non-streaming agents (extraction in particular) benefit from raw SDK error control and direct response inspection during structured-output parsing.
**Reasoning:** The two SDKs solve different problems and the design doc already calls out both. Cost is one extra dep. Forward-compat: if streaming becomes the right model for a currently-non-streaming agent (e.g. extraction gains incremental rendering in v1.5), migration is local to that agent file. SDKs deferred from Phase A per decisions.md 2026-05-11; install at the start of Phase B item 5 (synthesis), not before.

---

**Date:** 2026-05-13
**Decision:** Phase B shared-infra design: deterministic vault-context output, slug-based citation format, single UUID→slug index threaded to all serializers, insight framing modes, brief-exclusion as v1.5 forward-compat no-op
**Context:** Phase B items 1-4 ship the foundation every agent depends on (`lib/agents/_shared/{vault-context,errors,schemas,serializers/}`). Multiple design calls needed alignment before code lands — determinism contract for prompt caching, citation format alignment between Phase 5.3's outdated XML tag and Phase 10.3's markdown pills, insight framing distinct from Phase 5.6's anti-echo-chamber rule, slug derivation rules, and the brief-exclusion tripwire on a v1 entity that doesn't exist yet.
**Choice:** (1) `buildVaultContext` takes a third `now: Date = new Date()` parameter; same vault state + same `now` produces byte-identical output. Determinism is **per-request**, not across time — values like patient age update on subsequent runs but every call within a single agent invocation is consistent. No `new Date()` / `Date.now()` / `Math.random()` in the serializer + vault-context layer. Re-sort inside the serializer with `id` ASC tiebreaker. Omit empty sections entirely (no `# Allergies\n(none)`). Omit `createdAt` / `updatedAt` from output. (2) Citations use `§ entity-type:slug` markdown pill format per CLAUDE.md and 10.3:3102. The Phase 5.3 XML-tag example is stale; ignored. (3) Each serializer file exports `serializeXxx` + `xxxSlug`. `vault-context.ts` builds a single `Map<UUID, "entity-type:slug">` index once after loading entities and threads it into every serializer that emits cross-entity refs. Slug collisions get `-2`, `-3` suffixes in stable id-ASC order; semantic disambiguation (e.g. `med:amlodipine-5mg`) deferred to v1.5. (4) `serializeInsights` takes a mode parameter (`"deduplication-only"` vs `"full"`) and emits different framing preambles — the anti-echo-chamber rule from 5.6:889-895 is enforced at the vault-context layer, not buried in agent prompts. (5) `excludeBriefs` defaults true; v1 has no Brief entity, so the flag has no v1 data path. Comment marks the future entry point.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) Use UUIDs in citation pills directly (e.g. `§ med:8f3a2b1c-...`) — rejected, ugly AI output and breaks the readable-pill contract. (b) Build the slug index lazily inside each serializer — rejected, duplicates the loop and breaks single-source-of-truth for dedup suffixes. (c) Live with non-determinism (`updated_at` in output, no `id` tiebreaker) and accept cache misses — rejected, prompt caching is the v1 cost-optimization story for synthesis. (d) Inline the insight framing strings in agent prompts — rejected, vault-context is the load-bearing place to enforce framing because the same insight rows can be loaded by either synthesis or insight-gen with different framing needs.
**Reasoning:** The vault-context module is touched by every agent, so the constraints we lock here propagate everywhere. Determinism is a downstream investment: prompt caching only pays off when the cached prefix actually matches. Slug-based citations preserve readable AI output and let the citation parser (Phase C work in `components/ai-message.tsx`) resolve slug → entity at render time. The two-export-per-serializer pattern (function + slug) means serializers are pure and the slug index lives at the orchestrator layer where dedup can be centralized. Brief exclusion as a v1.5 tripwire is cheap insurance — when Brief becomes an entity we want the architectural guarantee already in place, not bolted on. Note: `server-only` was originally specified for `vault-context.ts` but dropped to enable tsx-based smoke tests; the credential boundary is at `@/db` / `@/lib/env` and `postgres-js` makes the dependency graph unbundleable for the client regardless. Documented inline in the file.

---

**Date:** 2026-05-13
**Decision:** Three follow-ups from /check review of Phase B items 1-4 — router confidence three-bucket lock, server-only placement deferred to Phase B cleanup with TODO, brief-exclusion regression assertion added per Phase 10.1 DoD
**Context:** /check audit of B1-B4 surfaced one spec inconsistency in the doc, one deferred refactor that should be tracked rather than left implicit, and one Phase 10.1 cross-cutting DoD item (10.1:2948) that wasn't covered by the initial smoke script.
**Choice:** (1) **Router confidence enum** — 5.5:833-837 prescribes `"high" | "medium" | "low"`; 10.3:3214 shows `"high" | "low"`. 5.5 is canonical (more considered specification; the 3-bucket model is genuinely useful for routing borderline inputs that don't warrant the explicit `ambiguous` intent). `lib/agents/_shared/schemas.ts` uses the 3-bucket version with an inline comment citing both spec locations. Doc fix to 10.3 pending — separate cleanup pass on `docs/design.md`. (2) **`server-only` boundary placement** — `vault-context.ts` keeps the dropped-guard pattern with an inline TODO comment marking Phase B cleanup (before Phase C) to push `import "server-only"` down to `@/lib/env`. Right moment to enforce is when the first client component starts importing from `@/lib/*`; bundleability via `postgres-js` is the de facto guard until then. (3) **Brief-exclusion regression assertion** — `scripts/check-vault-context.ts` now asserts that `excludeBriefs: true` and `excludeBriefs: false` produce identical output in v1. Tautological today (no Brief entity), load-bearing in v1.5 — fails loudly the moment Brief gets serialized without honoring the exclusion rule. Satisfies 10.1:2948.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) Resolve router confidence by following 10.3 (2-bucket) — rejected; 5.5 is the considered spec and the 3-bucket model serves real product needs. (b) Refactor `server-only` boundary now — rejected per user direction (don't refactor mid-implementation; finish what's in flight). (c) Skip the brief-exclusion test as tautological — rejected; 10.1:2948 explicitly asks for it and 5 lines is cheap insurance.
**Reasoning:** The router-confidence inconsistency is a real spec drift that needs resolving in code immediately (cheap) and in the doc later (separate pass). The `server-only` TODO captures the right cleanup boundary — Phase C is when client/server bundling actually matters; doing it earlier is busywork. The brief-exclusion assertion converts a memory-dependent invariant into a regression-protected one with negligible cost; the 10.1 DoD asked for exactly this and ignoring it would have left a latent gap that a v1.5 contributor could easily miss.

**Date:** 2026-05-14
**Decision:** Phase B item 5 — synthesis agent shipped with four locked patterns: verbatim prompt + runtime addendum, three-mode-detection-from-prompt, ephemeral cache control as single `system` SystemModelMessage breakpoint, end-to-end smoke gated on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` with verified cache hits
**Context:** Phase B item 5 per Phase 10.2; first agent built; carries the brand voice and hard rules for the rest of the agent family.
**Choice:** (1) **Verbatim prompt + addendum.** `SYNTHESIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT` is `docs/design.md` 10.3:3056–3177 character-for-character with no rewording. The doc's `[VAULT CONTEXT INSERTED HERE]` / `[OPTIONAL SURFACE CONTEXT TAG INSERTED HERE]` placeholders (3178–3184) are removed from the constant; runtime concatenation substitutes the real `buildVaultContext()` output (which itself appends the `<surface_context>` tag). A separate `PATIENT_NAMING_ADDENDUM` constant is appended after the verbatim prompt to resolve the schema gap at 3064 (no relationship-to-user field exists; verified by grepping `db/schema/*.ts` for relationship-carrying columns — `preferredName` is patient's own name, `family_history.relation` captures family-of-patient). The addendum explicitly prohibits inventing a relationship. (2) **Three modes, one prompt, no `mode` parameter.** Default chat / full health scan / investigate / doctor brief (delta + handoff) are all variant-detected by the model from the user turn per the prompt's "Capability variants" section. Function signature stays at `{ patientId, messages, surfaceContext? }`. Brief PDF download is a Phase D concern. (3) **Cache control wiring.** Single `system` `SystemModelMessage` (AI SDK v6 shape: `{ role: "system", content: string, providerOptions }`) with `providerOptions.anthropic.cacheControl: { type: "ephemeral" }`. This marks the full static-prompt + addendum + vault block as the cache breakpoint; the determinism contract baked into `buildVaultContext` (no embedded timestamps, sorted serializers, slug-index) is what makes the cached key byte-stable across turns. **End-to-end verified:** `scripts/check-synthesis.ts` runs two back-to-back calls and confirms call-2 reads ≥ call-1's `cache_creation_input_tokens` from cache. (4) **Smoke runner gated on env.** `npm run synthesis:check` exits 0 silently when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is unset (works in CI without the secret) and runs the full assertion otherwise.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) `mode: "chat" | "scan" | "brief"` parameter on `runSynthesis` — rejected; the design doc (5.7, 10.3:3130–3168) explicitly treats variants as model-side recognition, not caller-side switching. Same prompt, same architectural pattern. (b) Defer the smoke runner to Phase D when UI lands — rejected per user direction; smoke is the Phase 10.2 Phase B milestone ("hit /api/chat and get a streaming synthesis response from Opus reasoning over a real vault context"). Shipping the most-invoked agent without end-to-end verification would let agent bugs surface tangled with UI bugs later. (c) Multi-message system caching (two `SystemModelMessage` entries, cache control on the first) — rejected; the vault is dynamic-per-patient but byte-stable per chat session via the determinism contract, so a single end-of-system breakpoint covers it. The two-message pattern is only useful when you want to cache a small static prefix and skip caching a large dynamic suffix; we want the opposite. (d) Reading cache hits from `providerMetadata.anthropic.cacheReadInputTokens` directly — rejected; `@ai-sdk/anthropic` 3.0.x only surfaces `cacheCreationInputTokens` at the top level. The smoke script reads from `providerMetadata.anthropic.usage.cache_read_input_tokens` (raw snake_case) to get the real number. Pending upstream fix.
**Reasoning:** The verbatim prompt is load-bearing brand voice + hard rules; any paraphrasing risks losing the calibrated tone (3066–3074) or weakening guardrails (3092–3110). The addendum pattern keeps the verbatim constant auditable via simple diff against the doc while still resolving the relationship-field gap unambiguously. Three-mode-from-prompt matches the doc's architectural intent and keeps the API surface minimal — Phase D doesn't need to teach the chat UI about brief vs. scan vs. chat; it just sends the user's message. The cache control + smoke pairing is the payoff for last session's determinism work; verifying it now (3103-token cache hit confirmed) means we know prompt caching is wired correctly before scaling to insight-gen (item 6) which uses the same vault-context infrastructure. The gated smoke runner is the Phase B milestone; skipping it would defer real verification to Phase D and tangle agent bugs with UI bugs at the worst possible moment.

**Date:** 2026-05-14
**Decision:** Four /check follow-ups to Phase B item 5 — auth tripwire fix on `/api/chat`, spec-shaped error responses with `apiError()` helper, Zod request validation, prompt-byte-identity confirmed
**Context:** /check audit of the synthesis agent shipped earlier today surfaced four deviations from Phase 9.6 + CLAUDE.md tripwires — load-bearing for the v1.5 auth swap and for the next API route (item 6's insight-gen endpoint), so worth fixing before the pattern propagates.
**Choice:** (1) **Auth tripwire fix on `/api/chat`.** Removed `patientId` from the request body schema entirely; route now calls `getCurrentPatient()` per design.md 9.6:2755 + CLAUDE.md tripwire ("All auth flows through getCurrentUser/Patient"). The 9.3:2390 example that shows `patientId` in the body is illustrative; 9.6 is normative. With stub auth this was functionally equivalent to the prior code, but the v1.5 Supabase Auth swap relies on `getCurrentPatient()` being the only identity touch-point. (2) **Spec-shaped error responses.** New `lib/api/error.ts` exports `apiError(code, message, details?, statusOverride?)` returning `{ error: { code, message, details? } }` per 9.6:2782. The 4-value code enum (`validation_failed | not_found | unauthorized | server_error`) is treated as the public surface; AgentError-specific codes go in `details.agentCode`. `statusOverride` lets us return 429 for `rate_limit` while keeping `code: "server_error"` — Anthropic semantics surface in status, spec contract holds in shape. (3) **Zod request validation.** New `lib/schemas/api/chat.ts` exports `chatRequestSchema` per 9.6:2776-2777. Schema is loose on message content (`z.unknown()`) — Vercel AI SDK does the strict ModelMessage validation downstream; the route's job at the boundary is "is this even a chat request," not re-deriving the SDK's discriminated union. (4) **Verbatim prompt byte-identity confirmed and addendum tightened.** `SYNTHESIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT` now claims byte-identity against design.md lines 3056-3176 (was 3056-3177, off by one trailing newline). Verified by an ad-hoc diff script. `PATIENT_NAMING_ADDENDUM` got an explicit clarifier: "the examples in the prompt above that use 'your father' are illustrative of tone, not instructions to use that specific phrasing" — closes the gap where the model could mimic 3074/3118 examples despite the no-relationship rule.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) Keep `patientId` in body and rely on stub auth equivalence — rejected; the tripwire's whole point is that the v1.5 swap stays inside the helpers, and trusting client input for identity poisons that contract regardless of what stub auth happens to return. (b) Put `apiError` inside `lib/api/middleware.ts` per 9.6:2785 — rejected; middleware.ts per the spec is for "shared concerns (auth check, patient ownership check)," which is a different surface area. `lib/api/error.ts` is single-purpose and trivially importable. middleware.ts can land when item 6's route needs the patient-ownership check pattern. (c) Strict Zod validation of the full ModelMessage discriminated union — rejected; we'd duplicate the AI SDK's types and have to keep them in sync. Loose validation at the boundary + strict downstream is the right division. (d) Add a trailing `\n` to `SYNTHESIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT` to match the original 3056-3177 slice — rejected; the runtime composition adds `\n\n` separators around the addendum anyway, and shifting the slice end by one line is the cleaner way to claim byte-identity (the doc's blank line at 3177 is structural markdown spacing, not part of the prompt). (e) Drop the addendum's "examples are illustrative" line as overcautious — rejected; the verbatim prompt has two literal "your father" instances (3074, 3118) and one direct instruction at 3064. Without the clarifier, a model could plausibly read the examples as the canonical phrasing.
**Reasoning:** All four fixes are small (~50 lines total) and they set the right pattern for the next API route. The auth tripwire is the most load-bearing — `getCurrentPatient()` being the sole identity source is what makes Phase 9.6's v1.5 swap a one-helper change instead of a route-by-route migration. The error-shape contract is what gives the chat UI (Phase D) a stable error surface to render against; flat-vs-nested is the kind of thing that becomes a breaking change later if not locked now. Zod boundary validation matches the locked pattern in 9.6 and gives client-side errors a structured `details` field for actionable error messages. The prompt byte-identity claim is auditability — a future contributor can run the diff and verify zero drift; the addendum tightening removes a small but real failure mode (model copying "your father" from examples). Smoke still green: call-2 read 3150 tokens from cache after the addendum addition (was 3103 before the new clarifier line, now 3150 with it) — caching unaffected.

**Date:** 2026-05-15
**Decision:** Phase B scope corrected — items 1-7 only (Vault context + serializers + errors + Zod schemas + Synthesis + Router + Citation parser). Insight generator, Extraction, Onboarding, and Auto-titling moved out of Phase B and into Phase E ("AI-Driven Flows"). progress.md restructured to show Phase B → C → D → E → F sequence end-to-end.
**Context:** The original `progress.md` had Phase B as "items 1-10" — interpreting Phase B as "all 6 agents + 4 supporting infra files." Re-reading the design doc Phase 10.2 build sequencing surfaced that this conflates "all agents ship in v1" (true, per 10.1) with "all agents are buildable in Phase B" (false). Four of the six agents have hard dependencies on entity data and/or UI surfaces that don't exist until Phases C and D land.
**Choice:** Phase B = **agents and infra that have no dependency on entity data or surface UI.** Concretely: vault-context, serializers, errors, schemas, synthesis (works against an empty seed vault), router (pure classification, no vault context), and citation parser (pure markdown rendering). The remaining four agents move to Phase E: extraction (needs upload pipeline + confirmation surface from 6.11), onboarding (needs the live-populating right-panel surface from 6.3), insight generator (needs real entity data in the vault to surface patterns over — works against empty seed but produces nothing useful), auto-titling (needs the chat-history surface that Phase D builds). Phase D (replicate the Medication pattern across other state + event entities + patient profile + insights-feed placeholder) lands before Phase E so the AI flows have real data and surfaces to plug into. Phase F is polish.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) Build all 6 agents in Phase B as originally planned — rejected; building agents whose UI/data dependencies don't exist means writing code against speculative integration points, which forces rework when the surfaces actually land and makes the agents impossible to validate end-to-end (the synthesis-agent /check pass already showed how much value a real smoke test catches; we can't run an equivalent smoke for extraction without an upload UI, or for onboarding without the live-populating panel). (b) Delay Phase C until all agents are built — rejected; Phase C's vertical slice through Medication is what proves the wiring (entity → API → form → list → detail → chat → citation) works end-to-end. Front-loading agents would defer that proof and risk discovering wiring problems after 6 agents are written. (c) Build extraction in Phase B against a synthetic source (test fixture) — rejected; extraction's design (5.4) is tightly coupled to the confirmation flow (6.11) and the matching-dictionary pattern needs real entities to test the bucket logic against. Stub fixtures would mask exactly the integration risks we want to surface. (d) Treat the design doc's Phase numbering as authoritative and not restructure progress.md — rejected; the design doc's phases are a *capability* decomposition (5 = AI design, 6 = wireframes, 9 = architecture). progress.md's phases are a *build sequence*. They serve different purposes; conflating them is what produced the original 10-item Phase B.
**Reasoning:** The /check pass on synthesis (yesterday) made the cost of "infra without consumer" tangible — even a well-specified agent benefits enormously from a real end-to-end test against real surface integration. Building four more agents in that mode (no surfaces, no real data) would compound that cost four times over. By landing Router (small, no dependencies) and Citation parser (small, gates Phase C item 7) now, then pivoting to Phase C, we get the Medication vertical slice as a real proof point. Phase E then builds the remaining four agents against working surfaces and real data — extraction with the upload pipeline that exists, insight gen against a vault with actual medication data, onboarding against the live-populating right panel that's been built. This is the same logic as 10.2's build sequencing but specifically applied to the agent family. The doc-level Phase 5 (AI design) and 9.3 (AI integration) are unchanged — they describe what the agents *are*, not when they get built. progress.md is the operational sequencing layer; it's allowed to slice the work differently from the doc's capability decomposition.

**Date:** 2026-05-15
**Decision:** Phase B item 6 — Router agent shipped. Intent enum canonicalized to `question | log | ambiguous` (matches 5.5 + 10.3 verbatim); direct `@anthropic-ai/sdk` + prompted-JSON + Zod validation; defensive markdown-fence stripping in the parser; six-case env-gated smoke green. `9.3:2446`'s stale two-bucket `log | synthesis` framing added to the pending doc-fix list.
**Context:** Phase B item 6 per Phase 10.2. Smallest agent in the family; locks the patterns the remaining non-streaming agents (extraction, insight-gen, auto-titling — all Phase E) will follow.
**Choice:** (1) **Intent enum: `question | log | ambiguous`.** `question` describes what the user typed; `synthesis` is the destination agent, not the input shape. 5.5 + 10.3 both use `question`; 9.3:2446's `log | synthesis` is stale (uses destination-agent naming and drops the medium confidence bucket). Updated `routerOutputSchema` in the same patch. (2) **Direct `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, not Vercel AI SDK.** Matches 9.3:2407 ("Direct calls are simpler when streaming isn't needed; better control over timeouts, retries, error handling, structured output parsing"). Already in package.json from prior work. (3) **Prompted-JSON over tool_use forcing.** Anthropic has no native JSON mode; the canonical pattern is "tell the model in the prompt to return JSON only, parse + Zod-validate the text." Tool-use forcing works but adds ~200 tokens/call for a single-classification job. The Zod path catches drift. (4) **Defensive fence stripping.** First smoke run caught Haiku wrapping JSON in ```` ```json ```` fences on 4/6 cases despite explicit "no markdown fences" prompt instruction — well-known Haiku behavior. Added a `stripJsonFences()` regex helper that runs before `JSON.parse`. Re-run: 6/6 pass at ~1s per call. (5) **No prompt caching.** Router prompt is ~2KB, below Anthropic's ~1024-token caching floor — caching wouldn't fire. (6) **Empty-input short-circuits without API call.** Trimmed empty → `{ intent: "question", confidence: "low", reasoning: "empty input" }` directly; the prompt codifies this rule and there's no point paying for a round-trip. (7) **`AgentError` mapping for three real failure modes.** `RateLimitError` → `rate_limit` (recoverable), `APIConnectionTimeoutError` → `timeout` (recoverable), `JSON.parse` or Zod failure → `parse_failure` (not recoverable; same input will fail again), all other `APIError` + non-Error throws → `unknown`. `context_overflow` is not possible for the router (single tiny message) so unmapped. (8) **Signature: `{ input: string }`.** Object form matches `runSynthesis({ patientId, messages, surfaceContext? })`; leaves room to add parameters later without breaking callers. No `previousAssistantMessage` parameter — deferred until a real follow-up-question failure surfaces. No `surfaceContext` — router doesn't condition on surface per 5.5:822. (9) **Env-gated smoke runner.** `scripts/check-router.ts` mirrors `check-synthesis.ts`: skip-silently when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` unset, six sequential canonical cases (clear-question, clear-log, explicit-log, compound, empty, garbage), assert `intent` matches expected, print latency per case. Wired as `npm run router:check`.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) Keep `synthesis` in the intent enum — rejected per the user's reasoning ("the router classifies what the user typed, not which agent handles it"). (b) Vercel AI SDK uniformly across agents — rejected; 9.3:2407 explicitly splits streaming (AI SDK) from non-streaming (direct SDK), and direct calls give us per-error-class branching that's cleaner than the AI SDK's error wrapping. (c) Force structured output via `tool_use` with a single forced tool — rejected; adds ~200 tokens/call for a job that a clear prompt + Zod-validation handles. Revisit if Zod failures become frequent. (d) Cache the system prompt anyway — rejected; the prompt is well under Anthropic's caching floor, so it would just add token overhead with no cache hits. (e) Defer the smoke runner to Phase D when chat UI lands — rejected; same logic as synthesis smoke (Phase B item 5 decision) — agent bugs should surface before they tangle with UI bugs. Caught the fence-wrapping issue on the first run. (f) Strengthen the prompt to suppress markdown fences instead of stripping in the parser — rejected; Haiku's fence-wrapping behavior is documented and resistant to prompt fixes. Parser-side stripping is the standard fix, costs ~3 lines, and remains correct if the model ever stops wrapping. (g) Add a `previousAssistantMessage` parameter for follow-up disambiguation — explicitly asked; user confirmed defer. Worth revisiting if real chat usage surfaces follow-up classification failures. (h) Loose `garbage` assertion ("ambiguous OR confidence=low") — proposed proactively; not needed in practice (Haiku returned `ambiguous/low` cleanly on first try). Will weaken only if real flakiness emerges.
**Reasoning:** Router is the simplest agent in the family — small prompt, no vault context, single-message classification. The patterns locked here (direct SDK + prompted-JSON + Zod validation + AgentError mapping + defensive parser + env-gated smoke) are the template the remaining non-streaming agents (extraction, insight-gen, auto-titling — all Phase E) will follow. The fence-stripping discovery is the value of building the smoke alongside the agent: without it, the first real chat input would have hit a misleading "parse_failure" error in production. The `intent` enum canonicalization is small in code but load-bearing — it locks the public agent contract for everything downstream that reads router output (chat UI in Phase D, the compound-input flow in 5.5:846, the disambiguator surface for `ambiguous` inputs).

**Date:** 2026-05-16
**Decision:** Cleanup pass — applied four pending design.md doc-fixes to bring the spec into alignment with code that had already shipped. No code changes (other than removing now-stale "doc-fix pending" comments).
**Context:** The doc-fix list in `progress.md` had accumulated four entries — three from Phase B item 5 (synthesis) and one from item 6 (router) — each documenting a place where the design doc lagged a deliberate code decision. Items were non-blocking but the list was growing; cleaning them in a single pass before starting item 7 (citation parser) keeps the doc trustworthy for the next contributor reading 5.5/9.3/10.3.
**Choice:** Applied four doc edits to `docs/design.md`: (1) **3064 (relationship phrasing)** — clarified that the patient schema has no relationship-to-user field; "your father / your mother" is used only when the user states the relationship in conversation. Brings the verbatim prompt into alignment with the runtime addendum in `synthesis.ts` (`PATIENT_NAMING_ADDENDUM`). (2) **9.3:2395 + 2421 + 2436-2441 (stale model IDs)** — updated the streamText example, the extraction example, and the agent-model table to current "latest of tier" IDs per CLAUDE.md: Opus 4.5 → Opus 4.7, Sonnet 4.5 → Sonnet 4.6 (Haiku 4.5 already current). Header above the table now reads "latest of tier — keep IDs current per CLAUDE.md" so future contributors know the policy. (3) **10.3:3214 + 3217 (router output shape)** — changed intent enum to `question | log | ambiguous` (canonical per 5.5), confidence enum to three-bucket `high | medium | low`. Also fixed 3217 to say `→ question` instead of `→ synthesis` (the decision rule outputs the intent, not the destination agent), and rewrote 3218's compound-input bullet to make the "log first, synthesis after confirmation" flow explicit. (4) **9.3:2446 (router intent enum)** — same enum canonicalization as 10.3:3214, plus an inline parenthetical clarifying that `intent` describes user input, not the destination agent. Also cleaned up `lib/agents/synthesis.ts:140-146` and `lib/agents/_shared/schemas.ts:3-10` comments that referenced "doc-fix pending" — those references are now stale.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) Leave the doc-fix list growing and apply fixes at end-of-Phase-B as a single cleanup commit — rejected; growing the list past four items risks losing track of *why* each item was deferred, and the comments in code that reference "pending doc-fix" become harder to clean up the longer they sit (they accumulate version numbers and context). (b) Apply only the router-related fixes (item 6 ones) and leave synthesis-era fixes for later — rejected; the cleanup pass is cheaper as a batch than item-by-item, and the doc-trust value of a clean doc-fix list compounds across sessions. (c) Update CLAUDE.md's "still says 'Aarav' in places" note — deferred; grep returned no Aarav matches in design.md, so that note is itself stale, but editing CLAUDE.md is a different surface and worth surfacing separately rather than bundling into this cleanup.
**Reasoning:** Doc-code drift is the most expensive bug class in a heavily-referenced spec — every future contributor reads the doc and the code and has to reconcile any difference. Each of these four fixes was small (~1-5 lines), independently safe (doc-only or doc + a stale-comment cleanup), and individually justified by prior decisions (synthesis 5/4 and router 6). Doing them as a batch right before Phase B item 7 means the doc is in the right shape when the citation parser work needs to reference Phase 5.3 + 10.3 for citation format — and means the next time someone runs `/check` against Phase B, there are no "documented deviations" to read past.

**Date:** 2026-05-16
**Decision:** Phase B item 7 — Citation parser shipped. **Phase B is complete.** Three architectural choices: (1) inert pills only (popover deferred to Phase C item 7); (2) pure tokenizer in `lib/citations/parse.ts` separated from the remark plugin in `lib/citations/remark-plugin.ts` for testability; (3) `data-*` attributes on vault pills as the parser→Phase-C-popover handoff contract.
**Context:** Phase B item 7 per Phase 10.2. Last item before Phase C; closes the agent-infrastructure phase. Per the plan-mode discussion, two architectural questions resolved before coding: (a) shadcn/ui timing — explicitly deferred to Phase C per progress.md line 24; (b) parser implementation — `react-markdown` per 9.3:2444. The popover behavior (6.2:1206 + 6.2:1216) and the "grounded in →" footer (6.2:1207) were scoped out of Phase B because (i) shadcn/ui isn't initialized yet and (ii) there are no real entities to preview — both block on Phase C.
**Choice:** Five files. (1) **`lib/citations/parse.ts`** — pure `tokenizeCitations(input)` function with one regex alternation handling both vault and external citation forms. Vault: `§\s*([a-z][a-z0-9-]*)(?:\s*:\s*([a-z0-9/-]+))?` — matches type-only (`§ symptom-log`), with-slug (`§ med:amlodipine`), spaced-colon (`§ med: amlodipine`), and v1.5 date-suffix (`§ symptom: dizziness/apr-6` per 6.9:1634). External: `↗\s+([^.,;!?)\n§↗]+?)(?=\s*(?:[.,;!?)\n§↗]|$))` — terminates at sentence punctuation, `)`, newline, EOS, or the next citation glyph. The `\s*` in the lookahead absorbs trailing whitespace so adjacent externals (`↗ a ↗ b`) don't glue together. Adjacent text segments collapse via `pushText()`. (2) **`scripts/check-citation-parser.ts`** — 9 fixture cases + a round-trip assertion against `citationFor()` from `lib/agents/_shared/serializers/format.ts`. Pure unit test, no env gating, wired as `npm run citation-parser:check`. The round-trip lock means any future change to the serializer's pill format that breaks the parser fails loudly. (3) **`lib/citations/remark-plugin.ts`** — minimal unified/remark plugin using `unist-util-visit`. Walks `text` mdast nodes, skips `code`/`inlineCode` parents, delegates to `tokenizeCitations()`, and replaces matches with custom `citation-pill` mdast nodes carrying `data.hName: "citation-pill"` + `data.hProperties` (the React props). Uses TypeScript module augmentation to register the custom node in `mdast`'s `RootContentMap` (the canonical pattern for extending unist tree types) and intersects `hProperties` with hast's `Properties` so the index signature is satisfied without losing type-checked field names. (4) **`components/citation-pill.tsx`** — pure presentational `<span>` (not `<button>` — inert in v1, shouldn't claim focus/keyboard semantics). Two variants: vault (stone-100 background) and external (amber-50 background). Vault carries `data-citation-type="vault"`, `data-entity-type`, `data-slug`. External carries `data-citation-type="external"`, `data-source-name`. Phase C item 7 wraps with shadcn Popover by reading these attrs — no API change. (5) **`components/ai-message.tsx`** — `ReactMarkdown` wrapper with the citation plugin + components map. Sparkle marker (`✦` per 7.2:2149) on the left, markdown body on the right. The `Components` cast (`as unknown as Components`) bypasses react-markdown's strict `JSX.IntrinsicElements`-key restriction — documented community pattern for custom hast tag renderers. Re-exports `CitationPill` so Phase C item 7 imports from one surface.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) Pull shadcn/ui forward into Phase B item 7 to ship the full popover now — rejected; it pulls Phase C's "first component" milestone forward and the popover content (entity preview) needs real medications which don't exist yet. The clean Phase B/C split is more valuable than the temporary completeness. (b) Handwritten popover in Phase B, refactor to shadcn in Phase C — rejected; two implementations of the same behavior in two phases is waste. (c) Handwritten regex tokenizer instead of react-markdown — rejected; design.md 9.3:2444 names `react-markdown` explicitly, and a handwritten splitter would defer markdown rendering (bold/lists/headers from synthesis output) until later. (d) Visual smoke route at `app/dev/citation-test/page.tsx` — rejected; visual validation happens in Phase C item 7 when the chat surface lands. The unit test covers tokenizer correctness; visual correctness is a Phase C deliverable. (e) Augment `JSX.IntrinsicElements` globally for `citation-pill` — rejected for the React 19 + Next 16 module surface; the `as unknown as Components` cast is the documented react-markdown community idiom and changes one line vs. a global type-augmentation file. (f) Inline the tokenizer logic inside the remark plugin — rejected; separating `parse.ts` (pure) from `remark-plugin.ts` (mdast-coupled) lets the regex stay unit-testable from a tsx script with no unified deps in the test path. (g) Use `<button>` for vault pills with no onClick — rejected; affordances should match behavior, and a button without a click handler is misleading. The Phase C wrapper can swap to `<button>` when it adds the popover.
**Reasoning:** The parser is the consumer-facing edge of the synthesis-agent pipeline — every word the synthesis agent emits flows through it. Splitting the work as "Phase B = parser + visual contract; Phase C = wrapper + behavior" matches the actual seam in the work (regex/component vs. interaction/data-lookup) and matches the doc's seam between 9.3 (architectural placement) and 6.2 (interaction behaviors). The `data-*` attribute handoff is the load-bearing contract: Phase C's popover wrapper reads attrs from any vault pill in the DOM without re-parsing markdown or coordinating with Phase B's component API. The fence-stripping bug from item 6 is reprised here in spirit — the round-trip assertion against `citationFor()` is the equivalent insurance: when the serializer or parser changes, the smoke fails loudly before any production drift can happen. With Phase B closed, the next session pivots to Phase C's first vertical slice (Medication) — and the citation parser sits ready to render the demo moment's `§ med:amlodipine` pills the moment the chat UI renders synthesis output.

**Date:** 2026-05-16
**Decision:** End-of-Phase-B `/check` cleanup pass on the citation parser. Four code changes, two defer-with-comment notes, one skip.
**Context:** Principal-engineer review of Phase B item 7 surfaced one real spec deviation (typography classes referencing an uninstalled plugin), three small engineering tightens, and three Phase-C-handoff annotations. Cleanup landed before commit to keep the surface honest; the larger Phase C structural decisions (popover, brand palette, RSC client/server boundary) explicitly stay deferred.
**Choice:** (1) **Removed `prose prose-stone` from `components/ai-message.tsx`.** `@tailwindcss/typography` isn't installed; the classes were no-ops. Comment now explicitly punts markdown typography to Phase C item 7 — that surface owns the typography stack so all message types render consistently. Don't ship placeholder classes that don't work. (2) **Made `children` required on both `CitationPillProps` variants.** The remark plugin always emits a text child, so the optional typing was a lie about the runtime. Required-by-default forces consumers to be explicit. (3) **Extended round-trip smoke to cover the `unresolved:{uuid}` fallback path of `citationFor()`.** Now 11/11 cases (was 10/10). The unresolved path emits `§ unresolved:{uuid}` — UUID chars are all in the slug regex's `[a-z0-9/-]` class, so it tokenizes correctly today, but the assertion locks that behavior. If a future regex change excludes `-`, the smoke fails before production drift. (4) **Swapped external pill from `bg-amber-50` to `bg-stone-200`.** Amber leaned toward "startup-orange" which 7.2:2133 explicitly excludes. Both pill colors are now near-identical neutral grays — visually distinguishable enough for Phase B's no-consumer state, and the placeholder-only nature is more obvious since neither color "looks brand-correct." Comment in `citation-pill.tsx` calls out both as placeholders pending Phase C's earth-tone palette pick (7.2:2139). (5) **Added explicit "GLOBAL TYPE EXTENSION" note** to the `declare module "mdast"` block in `remark-plugin.ts`. Today only this file uses mdast types, but future code doing exhaustive `node.type` switches needs to handle `"citation-pill"` or explicitly opt out. (6) **Added "Server-Component-safe" docstring** to `ai-message.tsx`: `react-markdown` 10.x + `unist-util-visit` 5.x use no hooks/client APIs, so the file doesn't need `"use client"`. Phase C consumers can compose this inside client components without re-marking it. Cheap insurance against a contributor over-eagerly adding the directive. (7) **Skipped:** the `text` vs `raw` field-name inconsistency in `CitationSegment` — both names are semantically appropriate to their variant ("text content" for text segments; "raw matched citation" for vault/external). No churn.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) Install `@tailwindcss/typography` and add the v4 plugin directive — rejected; markdown typography is genuinely a Phase C concern (the chat surface owns the typography stack), and adding a plugin here would couple Phase B to a styling decision Phase C should make. (b) Keep amber as a "wrong on purpose" placeholder to force Phase C to repalette — rejected; Phase C's contributor reading code first might keep what's there, exactly the failure mode the principal-engineer review flagged. Two near-identical grays make the placeholder nature self-evident. (c) Remove the `CitationPill` re-export from `ai-message.tsx` (review item #4) — deferred; if it causes import-path confusion in Phase C, remove then. Not worth pre-emptive churn. (d) Make the round-trip test exhaustive (every entity-type the serializer might emit) — rejected; the two paths through `citationFor()` are resolved + unresolved, and asserting both is the minimum that catches behavior drift. Per-entity-type assertions would duplicate type-system coverage.
**Reasoning:** The `/check` discipline at phase boundaries earns its keep — the typography plugin gap and the children-typing lie would both have landed in Phase C's chat surface as confusing failures (markdown rendering would look wrong without typography; consumers would pass `children` thinking it was optional). Fixing them at the boundary keeps the inherited Phase B surface honest. The four defer-with-comment notes (#4 not done, #5 GLOBAL, #7 RSC, #6 placeholder) follow the same logic as the doc-fix list pattern: written-down deferrals don't decay; verbal "Phase C will handle this" notes do. With cleanup landed and Phase B item 7's smoke at 11/11 + typecheck + lint clean, the surface is ready for commit and pivot to Phase C item 1 (Medication API routes).

**Date:** 2026-05-16
**Decision:** Phase C item 1 (Medication API routes) shipped with four locked API conventions: (1) path-less routes — `patientId` always derives from `getCurrentPatient()`, never path/body/query; (2) PATCH refuses the four `medicationChangeField` enum fields and returns per-field guidance keyed by field name in `details`; (3) new `invalid_state_transition` (409) error code for domain state-machine violations; (4) transactional discontinue with `discontinuedOn` computed in the patient's IANA timezone via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`. `/check` review surfaced four engineering fixes that landed in the same commit (E1 enum drift, E2 timezone, E3 exhaustiveness, E4 auth ordering). Doc-fix pass applied alongside.
**Context:** First Phase C item; establishes the API conventions every subsequent entity route inherits (item 5 generic `/changes` POST, Phase D Conditions/Doctors/Allergies/Lifestyle CRUD). Plan-mode design call resolved the inline-edit-vs-change-log question by enforcing the CLAUDE.md tripwire ("a dose change appends a `medication_changes` row") at the API surface, not as a UI convention. /check ran immediately after the seven implementation steps landed; surfaced two real spec deviations (W1 path-less routes, W2 form-spec missing required-asterisks) and four engineering concerns (E1-E4 listed above).
**Choice:** (1) **Path-less routes** — `app/api/medications/{route,[id]/route,[id]/discontinue/route}.ts` instead of `/api/patients/[patientId]/medications/...`. Matches the 2026-05-14 auth-tripwire decision on `/api/chat`. design.md 9.6:2761-2768 updated to the path-less form with explicit note that v1.5/v2 multi-patient surfaces reintroduce `[patientId]` when sharing lands. (2) **PATCH refuses clinical-event fields** — `currentDose` / `currentFrequency` / `prescribingDoctor` / `status` all return 400 with `details.rejectedFields` keyed by field name pointing at the correct endpoint (`POST /changes` for the first three, `POST /discontinue` for status). The four fields ARE the `medicationChangeField` enum, so enforcing the rule via the schema (rather than UX convention) makes the data-architecture invariant provable from the API alone. Error responses become a teaching surface — concrete per-field guidance, not generic message + parenthetical. Saved as feedback memories `error-response-teaching-surface` + `state-transition-error-codes`. (3) **`invalid_state_transition` (409)** — new value in `ApiErrorCode` union with 409 Conflict status. Used for already-discontinued (now), already-resolved (Phase D Conditions), already-archived (future) cases. design.md 9.6:2782 updated to include the code and switch `details?: any` → `details?: unknown` for consistency. Includes `currentStatus` + `discontinuedOn` in details for client debugging. (4) **Transactional discontinue** — `medication_changes` insert + `medications` update inside `db.transaction(...)`. `discontinuedOn` computed via `new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-CA", { timeZone: patient.timezone, ... })` to honor 9.6:2803 ("Patient's local timezone, not the user's"). `getCurrentPatient()` extended to return `timezone: "Asia/Kolkata"` (matching seed). The helper signature takes `timezone` explicitly to keep the dependency visible at call sites. (5) **/check follow-ups:** E1 = Zod enums derive from Drizzle pgEnums (`z.enum(medicationStatus.enumValues)`) — locks single source of truth before items 2-7 propagate inline duplicates. E2 = the timezone fix above. E3 = `const _exhaust: never = err.kind` after the discontinue-route catch ladder — compile-time guarantee that future `MedicationDomainError` kinds get explicit routing. E4 = `getCurrentPatient()` moved to immediately-after-id-validation across PATCH/DELETE/discontinue (was after body parse) so v1.5 auth failures surface as `unauthorized` rather than `validation_failed`. (6) **Doc-fix pass:** design.md 6.12:1842 gets `*` on Dose / Frequency / Category (matches Phase 4 schema's `Req: yes`); 9.6:2761-2783 fully rewritten for path-less routes; CLAUDE.md stale "Aarav" note removed (grep confirmed zero matches in design.md per 2026-05-16 prior decision).
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **PATCH allows any field; UI enforces convention** — rejected; tripwire becomes a UX convention rather than enforced data-architecture rule. A future API caller (test script, second UI surface, automated job) could silently bypass change-log writes. (b) **PATCH on clinical fields auto-appends a change-log row** with `reason: null` — rejected; produces empty-reason entries that fail the demo-moment standard ("the dose was raised on April 3rd at the cardiology visit" needs `reason` + `linked_visit_id`); also conflates correction-style edits with clinical events. (c) **Use `validation_failed` for already-discontinued** — rejected; state-machine errors are semantically distinct from missing-required-field; forcing client code to branch on message strings is fragile, and 409 has the right HTTP semantics for client retry logic. (d) **`date-fns-tz` for timezone-aware dates** — rejected; `Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-CA", ...)` is built-in, produces YYYY-MM-DD natively, and adds zero deps. Forward-compatible: if a `lib/datetime.ts` helper consolidates this (per 9.6:2805), the call site moves. (e) **Query `patients.timezone` inside the discontinue transaction** instead of threading via `getCurrentPatient()` — rejected; less explicit, couples discontinue helper to the patient table, and `getCurrentPatient()` is the right single-source for all auth-relevant context (every state-transition helper will need this). (f) **Defer all /check fixes to a follow-up commit** — rejected; the four fixes are small (~80 lines combined) and locking the conventions (especially E1 enum drift) before items 2-7 inherit the wrong pattern is cheaper than fixing 5-7 files later. (g) **Doc-only `/api/patients/[patientId]/...` nesting kept as forward signal** — rejected; the doc and code should agree; the path-less route IS the v1 contract, and the v1.5 path will reintroduce nesting at that time.
**Reasoning:** Item 1 is the convention-locking commit for the entire Medication-style API surface; every entity in Phase D will copy this pattern. Spending the upfront cost to bake the tripwire into the API surface (PATCH-refuses-clinical) trades a little API ergonomics for an unforgeable data-architecture invariant — the only way to mutate dose/frequency/status is through the change-log-paired affordance. That invariant is exactly what the demo moment depends on: synthesis can cite `§ med:amlodipine` and accurately say "the dose was raised on April 3rd at the cardiology visit" because every dose change has a paired `medication_changes` row carrying `reason` + `linkedVisitId`. The timezone fix is small but demo-relevant: a Pune-evening discontinue would have stored yesterday's UTC date and made synthesis cite the wrong day. The enum-drift fix (E1) is the highest-leverage cleanup — applied to Medication enums alone it saves 24 lines, but the convention propagated across all 14 entities prevents drift across the entire codebase. /check immediately after the implementation pass earned its keep here: caught the timezone bug (demo-impacting), the enum drift (highest propagation cost), and the auth-ordering inconsistency (v1.5 behavior cliff) before any of them touched a second route.

**Date:** 2026-05-16
**Decision:** Phase C item 2 (shadcn/ui init + Medication form) shipped. Four design decisions made up-front: (1) palette = muted sage on warm-stone neutrals (resolves the 6.12 "terra/clay" conflict against 7.2's Claude-collision rule); (2) `prescribingDoctor` + `purpose` linked-entity fields deferred to Phase D when the parent entities and their autocomplete + inline-modal pattern can be built properly; (3) `brandName` added to the form despite being absent from 6.12's field list (Phase 4 schema has it as useful in India; cheaper to surface now than retrofit later); (4) interim minimal medications list page shipped so Save/Cancel flows have somewhere to navigate — full 6.4 state-list template defers to item 3. Also: `import "server-only"` pushed down to `lib/env.ts` (the canonical credential boundary) per the long-standing TODO in `vault-context.ts`; the form is the first client component to import from `@/lib/*` and the trigger condition for that move. shadcn/ui v4.7 + Tailwind 4 + React 19 init with Base UI primitives (modern preset replaced legacy Radix-based shadcn).
**Context:** First user-facing surface in the app; the form template per design.md 6.12 is replicated by every other state entity in Phase D, so its structure is load-bearing well beyond medications. Plan-mode flagged four open questions (palette / linked-entity fields / brandName / post-save nav) — user approved all four recommendations. Two user-requested refinements applied: (a) curl-smoked the create endpoint with three invalid payloads before relying on the error shape from form side — confirmed `body.error.details = { formErrors, fieldErrors }` matches Zod's `.flatten()` output, `.strict()` violations route to `formErrors` (not `fieldErrors`), and non-JSON bodies omit `details` entirely; (b) added an explicit one-line comment in the form noting that the `Started on` field's native-date-input + browser-locale rendering is *intentional* for a date column (calendar day, not moment) and the default-today value is computed in the patient's IANA timezone per 9.6:2803.
**Choice:** (1) **Palette = sage on stone.** `globals.css` overrides shadcn's default `neutral` (chroma-0 grays) with warm-tinted neutrals at hue ~80 (`--background: oklch(0.985 0.004 80)`, `--foreground: oklch(0.25 0.012 70)`, etc.) and `--primary` as muted sage at hue ~155 (`oklch(0.5 0.055 155)`). Dark-mode mirrors with inverted lightness, same hues. `--destructive` stays desaturated red (`oklch(0.55 0.18 27)`) per 7.2's "restrained severity colors." `--accent` is sage-tinted muted (hue 155, low chroma 0.012) so component focus states feel coherent. The citation-pill placeholder colors (`stone-100` / `stone-200`) are explicitly left alone in this commit — they're Tailwind stone, which is hue-matched-enough to the new tokens that the pills don't clash, and the migration to `--muted` / `--secondary` tokens belongs to Phase C item 7 when the popover wrapper lands. (2) **Skip linked-entity fields.** Form omits both `prescribingDoctor` and `purpose` from the rendered fields; Zod schema accepts them as optional and the API stores `null`. Phase D adds them with the full 6.12 spec (rich autocomplete from the patient's doctors/conditions + `+ Create new` inline-modal). No disabled-with-tooltip half-state — it would telegraph the eventual shape but adds visual debt for a feature that's a phase away. (3) **`brandName` included paired with Name in a 2-col grid.** design.md 6.12:1842 updated to list Brand name explicitly. The schema column already existed (Phase 4); 6.12 just hadn't surfaced it. Retrofit risk: medications entered via the form in this phase would lack brand_name even if users wanted to capture it. Better to surface now. (4) **Interim list page.** `app/patient/[id]/medications/page.tsx` ships as a thin server-rendered list (name + dose + frequency + status, no card styling, no filters, no sections) with `+ Add medication` link. Comment at top names it interim and points at item 3 for the full 6.4 template. This file becomes the seed for item 3 — expanded, not deleted. (5) **shadcn init details.** `npx shadcn@latest init -d` (defaults preset, `base-nova` style, neutral base — then re-tuned to stone/sage in globals.css). `components/ui/{button,input,label,textarea,select,form,alert-dialog,field,separator}.tsx` added. The legacy `form` primitive (RHF-coupled) is replaced in base-nova by the lower-level `field` primitive family (`Field`, `FieldLabel`, `FieldError`, etc., unstyled wrappers); RHF wires up via `useForm` + `Controller` directly, not through a shadcn-provided form-context. `react-hook-form` + `@hookform/resolvers` installed as direct deps. (6) **Form-side Zod schema duplication.** `components/medications/medication-form.tsx` defines a permissive `formSchema` that mirrors required fields from `createMedicationSchema` but allows empty strings on optional text fields (`brandName`, `startedOn`, `notes`). At submit, empty strings are stripped to `undefined` before the POST so the server's stricter `.min(1)` schema accepts them as omitted. Enum value-sets imported from the Drizzle pgEnums (`@/db/schema`) — single source of truth, no enum drift between client and server. Comment in the form file documents the divergence and points at the server schema as authoritative. (7) **Server-only refactor.** `lib/env.ts` gets `import "server-only"` as its first import. The `vault-context.ts` TODO comment collapses to a one-line pointer at lib/env as the canonical boundary. Three current importers of `@/lib/env` (`lib/supabase.ts`, `db/seed.ts`, `db/index.ts`) all server-side — grep-verified pre-edit. (8) **Cancel-with-unsaved-changes.** Cancel button checks `form.formState.isDirty`; empty form navigates directly, dirty form opens `<AlertDialog>` with title `Discard your changes?` body `If you cancel now, the medication won't be saved.` and actions `Keep editing` / `Discard` (destructive variant). Microcopy per 7.1 voice — warm, plain, direct. (9) **Two save modes.** Primary `Save` (form submit) navigates to the interim list page on 201 + calls `router.refresh()`. Secondary `+ Save and add another` (type="button", outline variant) calls the same submit handler with mode flag, then `reset(defaultValues)` on success. Both share validation error → RHF `setError` mapping + top-of-form banner for `formErrors` or non-validation errors.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **Stick with shadcn's default neutral palette** — rejected; chroma-0 grays clash with 7.2's "warm-tinted neutrals" directive. The override is small and isolated to `globals.css`. (b) **Ship `prescribingDoctor` / `purpose` as disabled selects with "Add a doctor first" tooltip** — rejected; telegraphs eventual shape but adds visual debt for a feature one phase away; cleaner to omit entirely and add the full pattern when entities ship. (c) **Build `/api/doctors` + `/api/conditions` GET endpoints and ship plain native selects** — rejected; that's a Phase D crossover that pulls scope forward without delivering the proper 6.12 autocomplete + `+ Create new` pattern, and unseeded lists render empty anyway. (d) **Omit `brandName` per 6.12 literal** — rejected; the field is in the Phase 4 schema for a reason (India brand-name dominance), retrofitting forms across all entities later is more expensive than surfacing now. (e) **Inline-modal flash + stay on form for primary Save** — rejected; the user explicitly expected navigation, and the interim list page is cheap. (f) **Navigate to `/` on Save** — rejected; user loses the medication they just created since there's no other surface yet. (g) **Use legacy `shadcn add form`** — not available in base-nova; the modern preset is base-nova-only at this point, and base-nova ships `Field` instead. (h) **Share `createMedicationSchema` directly client-side (no form-side schema)** — rejected; `.min(1).optional()` rejects empty strings on the resolver path, RHF doesn't preprocess to undefined natively, and the duplication is small + commented. The server schema stays the authoritative tightest version. (i) **Move post-save flash via shadcn toast/sonner** — deferred; not a Phase C item 2 dependency, and `router.refresh()` after navigation gives a perfectly fine feedback shape (the user lands on the list and sees the new row). (j) **Render the page shell title + Cancel button entirely server-side and pass form-dirty state via context** — rejected; over-engineering for a single check, simpler to render title + Cancel inside the client form component.
**Reasoning:** Item 2 is the first surface and lays template for every other entity form in Phase D. Three things had to land right: the visual baseline (palette + shadcn init), the form template (per 6.12, replicable per-entity), and the scope discipline (don't pull Phase D crossover work just because a related entity exists). The skip-linked-fields decision is the scope-discipline lever — adding doctors/conditions partial UI would have produced ~3 entity-types' worth of half-baked surface in this session, and the Phase D template is already prescribed by 6.12 itself. The form-side schema duplication is the one piece that introduces drift risk; the comment + the shared pgEnum import + the server schema staying authoritative means the worst case is the form accepting one extra valid edge case that the server then rejects — a 400 banner, not silent data corruption. The interim list page is the trick that unblocks end-to-end testing: without it Save would have to either flash-and-stay (diverges from 6.12) or navigate-to-nowhere — the bare list is ~25 LOC and becomes the file item 3 expands. The server-only push to lib/env is the right moment now that a client component depends transitively on enum types from `@/db/schema` — moving the guard to the canonical layer means a future contributor who imports env in a client component fails at build time with a meaningful error, not at runtime with a Postgres handshake mystery. The user's two refinements — pre-smoking the API error shape, and the date-input intentional-tz comment — both protect against future contributor confusion: (a) the form's error-mapping code is now built against an *observed* contract rather than an assumed one; (b) the comment on `startedOn` documents the date-vs-moment distinction at the exact place a future contributor would otherwise think the code is broken.
