**Date:** 2026-05-18
**Decision:** Phase C item 2 `/check` cleanup pass. Nine fixes landed (W1, W2, W3, E1, E3, E4, E5, E6, E7). One real spec deviation, six engineering tightens, one cosmetic, one dependency-section fix.
**Context:** Principal-engineer review after the item 2 implementation surfaced one spec deviation (form schemas inlined in the component file instead of `lib/schemas/forms/` per 9.6:2796), one visual-treatment inversion against the spec (label uppercase vs helper uppercase), six engineering concerns (factory pattern, setError whitelist, body declarative-ness, datetime dedup, unused palette tokens, CLI in production deps), and one breadcrumb micro-deviation. Item 2 ships the template every Phase D entity form copies, so locking the right conventions before propagation is high-leverage.
**Choice:** (1) **W1+E2 — form schema relocation + API-schema derivation.** Created `lib/schemas/forms/medication.ts` exporting `medicationFormSchema = createMedicationSchema.extend({ brandName, startedOn, notes })`. Required-field constraints (name, currentDose, currentFrequency, category) now inherit from the API schema — a rename or new required field in `createMedicationSchema` surfaces as a TS error in the form, not a runtime mismatch. The `.extend()` override on optional text fields is the intentional permissive layer: RHF inits string inputs with `""`, the API rejects `""` via `.min(1)`, and the submit handler coerces `""` → `undefined` so the wire body matches the strict contract. (2) **W2 — label/helper visual inversion.** Per 6.12:1826 (`FORM · default Tablet`), field labels render uppercase-mono-tracking-wide-text-xs and helpers reset to `font-sans font-normal normal-case`. Achieved via `FIELD_LABEL_CLASS` applied to every `<FieldLabel className={...}>` and a `LabelHelper` that explicitly resets case + tracking + weight inside the cn-merged label scope. Also added the missing "defaults to today" helper to the Started On field (the spec lists both `defaults to today` and `native picker` — they coexist, separated by `·`). (3) **W3 — breadcrumb cleanup.** Added leading `/` per 6.12:1833 (was missing). Patient ID still truncated to first 8 chars + ellipsis; rationale now lives in an inline comment in both `new/page.tsx` and `medications/page.tsx`. (4) **E1 — flatten submit factory.** The old `submit(mode)()` factory-returning-factory pattern collapsed into two distinct named handlers (`onSubmitSave`, `onSubmitAddAnother`) sharing a single `postMedication(values)` helper that returns `boolean`. Side-effect divergence (navigate vs reset) lives at the call site, not threaded through a mode flag. Phase D entity forms will copy this pattern. (5) **E3 — setError whitelist.** Server `fieldErrors` keys are now matched against `formKeys = new Set(Object.keys(defaultValues))` before calling `setError`. Unknown keys collect into `unknownFieldMessages[]` and append to the banner alongside `formErrors`, so a server-validated field the form doesn't render (e.g. an optional linked-entity field once Phase D wires them) surfaces as a banner error instead of silently landing on an invisible field. (6) **E4 — declarative POST body.** Replaced the imperative `Record<string, unknown>` + if-ladder with a single declarative object using `value?.trim() || undefined` on optional fields. `JSON.stringify` drops `undefined` values natively, so the wire format is identical while the type contract stays preserved. (7) **E5 — `lib/datetime.ts` extracted.** Created the file design.md 9.6:2805 names as the canonical date-helper location, seeded with `todayInTimezone(timezone)`. Replaces three lines of inline `Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-CA", ...)` in both `app/patient/[id]/medications/new/page.tsx` and `db/queries/medication.ts` discontinue. Comment at the top documents why date-fns isn't pulled in for this one helper (built-in Intl is sufficient; date-fns lands when display-side formatters do). (8) **E6 — strip unused palette tokens.** Deleted all `--sidebar-*` and `--chart-*` CSS variables from both `:root` and `.dark` in `app/globals.css`. The shadcn defaults were chroma-0 grays plus `oklch(0.488 0.243 264.376)` (saturated blue) for `--sidebar-primary` in dark mode — that blue is exactly the anti-pattern 7.2:2131 ("Not a clinical-blue palette") explicitly rules out. Comment in the `@theme inline` block notes that sidebar/chart tokens land palette-aligned when the first consumer ships. (9) **E7 — shadcn → devDeps.** `npm uninstall shadcn && npm install --save-dev shadcn`. The shadcn CLI is a build-time tool; production runtime never reaches for it. Required clearing `.next/` and restarting the dev server to clear stale module-resolution cache after the npm reshuffle.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **Keep the inline `formSchema` in the form component** — rejected; design.md 9.6:2796 is explicit ("Form schemas live in `lib/schemas/forms/`") and Phase D will copy this form 5+ times. Locking the wrong convention now is expensive. (b) **Override `<FieldLabel>` once globally with `text-xs uppercase` shadcn primitive customization** — rejected; the primitive is shadcn-managed, modifying it directly diverges from upstream and complicates future shadcn updates. Pass-through className via `FIELD_LABEL_CLASS` is the documented shadcn customization pattern. (c) **Keep the `submit(mode)` factory pattern with a clearer comment** — rejected; two named handlers are objectively clearer and the cost is identical line count. Phase D forms will copy whichever pattern lands here. (d) **Move the breadcrumb to a shared component** — deferred; one duplication isn't enough to extract. When Phase D adds breadcrumbs to 5+ pages, extract then. (e) **Skip E5 (datetime extraction) — only two call sites today** — rejected; Phase D's 4 state entities each ship a discontinue/resolve/archive action + create form, all of which need today-in-tz. Three call sites is the right extraction moment per the rule of three; deferring to four-plus is just more migration cost. (f) **Keep the unused palette tokens with a "TODO retune" comment** — rejected; the saturated blue is the exact 7.2-anti-pattern that the principal review caught, and a contributor adding a sidebar component would inherit it silently. Deleting forces an explicit re-add at consumer time with the palette in mind. (g) **Defer E7 (shadcn → devDeps)** — rejected; production bundles picking up a CLI tool is straight bloat, the move is one command, and the CSS import path quirk (`shadcn/tailwind.css` requires the package to be installed in `node_modules`, which devDeps does for dev but not for prod) is solved at the same time by Next's CSS-import resolution working off `node_modules` either way during build.
**Reasoning:** The principal-review checkpoint after item 2 caught two classes of issue: (a) one real spec deviation (`lib/schemas/forms/` location) that would have propagated to every Phase D form if left alone; (b) seven engineering tightens that would have been "we should clean this up" technical debt forever. The cost to fix at this checkpoint is small (~30 minutes total), the cost after Phase D ships 5 similar forms is 5× higher — both for the engineering work and for the reviewer-attention surface. The W2 visual inversion is the only fix that's purely visual; the rest are either contract-tightening (W1+E2 derivation, E3 whitelist, E5 dedup), correctness-edge (E4 declarative body removes one source of stringly-typed mutation), or dependency hygiene (E6 dead tokens, E7 wrong section). The `/check` discipline at phase-item boundaries continues to earn its keep — these issues all became visible only after the implementation was complete, which is exactly when the second pass should happen.

**Date:** 2026-05-20
**Decision:** Palette reset — stone-only neutrals for Phase C items 2-4. Brand accent for primary actions deferred to Phase C checkpoint 1 (after items 3-4) when multiple surfaces exist to validate candidates against.
**Context:** Visual review of the first-pass Medication form (item 2) flagged the Save / `+ Add medication` buttons as too green. Initial implementation reached for `bg-emerald-700` (assistant-supplied default) before the `/check` correction; the planned recommendation that replaced it was muted sage at `oklch(0.5 0.055 155)`. Both registers read as wellness-green against 7.2 anti-patterns ("Not a wellness-green palette," "No green for 'positive feedback' semantics") even at desaturated chromas. The third candidate evaluated this session — dusty seafoam, recommended by the original plan-mode options for item 2 — would have hit the same anti-pattern. Three different green-adjacent picks, three rejections against the same constraint.
**Choice:** (1) **Phase C items 2-4 use stone-only neutrals.** `--primary` set to Tailwind stone-700 (`oklch(0.374 0.01 67.558)`), `--primary-foreground` to stone-50 (`oklch(0.985 0.001 106.423)`), `--ring` to stone-500, `--accent` / `--accent-foreground` to stone-100 / stone-700. Dark mode mirrored: stone-300 / stone-900 for primary; stone-800 / stone-200 for accent; stone-400 for ring. Sage hue 155 fully removed from the palette. (2) **Brand accent deferred to Phase C checkpoint 1** (after items 3-4 land — the full list page and detail page surfaces). Rationale: picking an accent in isolation against a single form page has produced three failed candidates; three surfaces (form + list + detail) make the right pick obvious where one surface does not. (3) **design.md 6.12:1821 neutralized** to "warm dark neutral (stone-700 or equivalent)" with the deferral note pointing here. (4) **Cancel button semantics** flipped from `variant="ghost"` (button-shaped frame) to `variant="link"` (text-link with hover underline) to match the user's "not a button, just a text link" framing. The link variant uses `text-primary` which is now stone-700, so Cancel reads as a quiet warm-dark text link, no green tint.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **Pick a fourth candidate (muted terracotta, warm brown, aged linen) now and commit** — rejected; the failure mode isn't "we picked the wrong shade," it's "we don't have enough surface area to evaluate." Phase 7.2 lists six earth-tone candidates and the right one likely emerges when looking at three+ surfaces in concert, not one. (b) **Use a non-color accent — bold weight, underline, or larger radius on primary buttons** — rejected; this is structural debt that gets unwound when an accent finally lands, and meanwhile the warm-dark stone-on-cream reads as confident click-me without needing color semantics. (c) **Keep sage but desaturate further (chroma 0.02)** — rejected; chroma reduction toward 0 reads as muddy gray-green rather than removing the green register, and the principal review correctly identified that "low-chroma green" still triggers the 7.2 anti-pattern at the perception level. (d) **Defer items 3-4 until accent is locked** — rejected; the spec is that stone-only is a valid v1 baseline, and items 3-4 don't depend on accent for their structural design — they need cards, sections, filter pills, header treatments, all of which the current palette supports.
**Reasoning:** This is the third palette deferral on this codebase (dusty seafoam in the original item 2 plan options, sage in the recommended plan, emerald in first-pass implementation). The pattern is informative: every individual pick has read wrong because 7.2's anti-patterns ("not clinical-blue, not wellness-green, not startup-orange/terra/clay, not playful, not minimalist-austere") describe a fence around a small space, and inside that space the right pick depends on system-wide validation, not single-surface preview. Locking a stone-only baseline buys two things: (a) items 2-4 ship without the palette decision blocking them, and (b) by the time three surfaces exist, the accent decision becomes a comparison problem (which of these candidates harmonizes across all three?) rather than a generation problem (what would work here?). The meta-lesson for future palette work: **the system is asking for an accent but the constraint is a multi-surface comparison constraint, not a hue constraint**. Checkpoint 1 (post-items-3-4) is the canonical retry. **Allowed at checkpoint 1:** earth-tone family per 7.2:2139 — muted terracotta, dusty rose, warm brown, aged linen, non-green eucalyptus. **Explicitly disallowed:** any pick in the wellness-green register, regardless of how desaturated; sage, seafoam, eucalyptus that leans green, mint, jade. The constraint is perceptual ("does this read as a health/wellness app stereotype?"), not technical (chroma threshold).

**Date:** 2026-05-27
**Decision:** Lock the Phase C item 3 list-template pattern + capture four forward-looking deviations from design.md 6.4 that Phase D will replicate.
**Context:** Phase C item 3 (Medications list page) ships the state-list template per design.md 6.4 — the same template Phase D replicates for Conditions, Doctors, and Family history. Decisions made here propagate to four more entity surfaces. Plan-mode produced eight tactical decisions (D1-D8); four of them have forward implications worth recording. The other four (filter state location, link-wiring scope this round, inert AskAI button, subtitle zero-omit) are implementation details documented in the plan + code.
**Choice:** (1) **`medication-card.tsx` extracted as a standalone RSC**, not inlined in the page. Phase D entities each get their own `*-card.tsx` file with a `{ patientId, entity, ...linked refs }` prop shape. (2) **PAUSED enum gets its own collapsed-by-default group** between ACTIVE and DISCONTINUED when `pausedCount > 0`. `Active only` filter excludes PAUSED. (3) **Recent-change indicator (`[↑ recent change]` per design.md 6.4:1305) deferred** until usage data tells us what "recent" should mean — TODO comment in `medication-card.tsx`; revisit when at least one entity has months of change history accumulated. (4) **Empty-state copy trimmed from verbatim 6.4:1350** — "No medications added yet — add manually below." replaces the verbatim "let arogya extract them from a prescription photo, or add manually" because extraction is Phase E and the build sequence inverted the spec's assumption. TODO + comment in `medication-empty-state.tsx`; restore the full phrasing once Phase E ships extraction.
**Alternatives considered:** (1) **Inline card markup in the page** — rejected; Phase D would either copy-paste the inline JSX into four other pages or land a refactor later. Both worse than a 10-line standalone-file extraction now. The "extract when it grows" heuristic almost always means "never extract." (2) **Bucket PAUSED under ACTIVE** — rejected; semantically misleading and breaks filter semantics ("Active only" would still surface paused medications, which is wrong). The alternative was also discarded by spec: 6.4 only enumerates ACTIVE and DISCONTINUED for Medications, but the schema enum includes PAUSED and we need a coherent rendering for it. (3) **Ship a 14-day recent-change window now** — rejected; commits to a product call (what time window is informative vs noisy) without usage data, and that commitment propagates to all four Phase D entities the moment the template gets copied. (4) **Ship the verbatim 6.4 empty-state copy** — rejected; the spec wording is correct in steady state but advertises a feature (extraction) that doesn't exist yet, creating a phantom-promise UX issue. Voice rule "never make a promise we can't keep" outweighs spec verbatim-ness when the spec assumed sequencing we didn't follow.
**Reasoning:** Phase C item 3 is the template that Phase D copy-pastes four times. Decisions here have a 5× multiplier on downstream cost — bad calls compound. Standalone card files are the cheapest preemptive abstraction in the codebase (10 lines moved); the alternative is paying the refactor four times in Phase D. PAUSED-as-its-own-group is the semantically correct rendering and prevents the filter from silently misbehaving the moment any user pauses a medication. Recent-change deferral and the empty-state copy revision are both responses to spec assumptions that didn't survive contact with our actual build order (extraction comes in Phase E, not before Phase C). Both have explicit restore-conditions documented in the code so future-me knows when to revisit. Implementation details that didn't earn an entry: hybrid filter state (URL params for status/category + useState for collapse — direct application of 6.4's "state persists within the session"), card→detail link wiring this round (404 acceptable; cleaner than inert-then-wire), inert AskAI button with `aria-disabled` (machine-readable signal without "coming soon" copy), subtitle zero-omit ("0 active · 5 discontinued" reads weird).

**Date:** 2026-05-28
**Decision:** Lock Phase C item 4 scope (Medication detail page) with six scoping calls; defer Edit + clinical-field editing to item 5
**Context:** Phase C item 4 implements design.md §6.5 state entity detail template — the canonical pattern Phase D will mirror four times (Condition, Doctor, Allergy, LifestyleProfile/FamilyHistory). Item 5 explicitly owns inline editing and the `+ Log a change` form. Several scoping questions: what chrome to render now vs. defer; how to handle Linked Context when cross-entity pages don't ship until Phase D; how to size the History collapse threshold; how to handle 409 invalid_state_transition on Discontinue; whether to seed a synthetic creation row on medication insert; what date formatters to add.
**Choice:** (1) **No Edit button in item 4.** Item 5 introduces inline editing + the Edit affordance simultaneously — no inert placeholder that misleads. (2) **`…` menu contains Discontinue only** (Delete and Export deferred); menu is **omitted entirely** when `status === "discontinued"` (single-item menu with no useful action). Item 5's Edit makes the menu non-empty regardless of status — pattern stabilizes then. (3) **Linked Context renders visit references from `medication_changes.linkedVisitId` rows only.** Pills inert (no `<Link>`) until Phase D visit pages exist. Doctor + Condition stay in the Current grid, not duplicated into Linked Context. Section **omitted entirely when zero linked visits** (per §6.5 LifestyleProfile precedent). (4) **History collapse only when `changes.length > 5`.** Most meds have 0-3 changes; rendering a `Show all 3 changes ▾` button is noise. Empty-history renders `No changes logged yet.` inline. (5) **`shadcn dropdown-menu` primitive added now** to amortize Phase D — Delete/Export/Edit land in items 5+ and Phase D each adds 1-2 menu items per entity. (6) **No synthetic creation row in `medication_changes`.** Audit log records *changes*, not row-creation; `medication.startedOn` already captures the start. Synthesizing an initial row would conflate row-creation with clinical events. (7) **Notes section omitted when empty** (mirrors Linked Context's pattern). §6.5 does not yet carve this out — flagged as pending doc-fix; spec should permit Notes omission to keep detail pages quiet when there's nothing to say. (8) **409 invalid_state_transition path replaces Discontinue button with OK** that calls `router.refresh()` and closes the dialog. Disabling Discontinue would strand the user staring at a dialog they can't resolve cleanly; the OK→refresh path acknowledges the page state is stale and brings the UI in line with reality. (9) **Three datetime display helpers added to `lib/datetime.ts` without `date-fns`** — `formatAbsoluteDate(d)`, `formatRelative(d, now?)`, `formatRelativeAndAbsolute(d, now?)`. Plain `Intl` + arithmetic (~30 LOC); revisit the date-fns dep when ≥6 surfaces format dates and an awkward edge case appears (calendar-aware month arithmetic, DST in display logic, etc.). Change-entry rail stacks relative over absolute per §6.5:1411.
**Alternatives considered:** (1) Render Edit as inert placeholder for visual layout consistency — rejected, misleads users into clicking a button that doesn't work. (2) `…` menu contains Discontinue + Delete — rejected for item 4; Delete is destructive and warrants its own UX pass (confirmation, what cascades, can history be recovered). Item 5 will revisit. (3) Linked Context rendered with placeholder copy (`Linked entries will appear here when…`) — rejected; same polish-debt class as the Phase E extraction promise we already pulled out of the empty-state. (4) Collapse always (`changes.slice(0, 5)` + show-all even at 5) — rejected; renders noise for the 95% case of low-change-count meds. (5) Plain icon button → dialog (no dropdown shell since only one menu item) — rejected; Phase D adds Delete/Export per entity, paying the primitive cost twice. (6) Seed a synthetic `field: "dose"` row on `medication.create` with `oldValue: null, newValue: currentDose` — rejected; pollutes the audit-log boundary, requires `medicationQueries.create` change. (7) Render Notes empty-state copy (`No notes yet.`) — rejected on noise grounds. (8) Disable the Discontinue button on 409 with an in-dialog explanation — rejected; user can't resolve cleanly without refreshing. (9) Add `date-fns` per design.md 9.6:2798 — rejected for now; the four-helper surface is small and `Intl.DateTimeFormat` produces the same output natively. The 9.6 prescription is non-binding once one helper proves a dep isn't needed; revisit at the next genuinely awkward date case.
**Reasoning:** Phase C item 4 is the template that Phase D copies 4-6 times — bad calls here compound. Decisions favor clean scope boundaries between item 4 and item 5 (Edit/Delete defer cleanly), avoid noise in the rendered surface (collapse only when needed, omit Notes/Linked Context when empty), and protect semantic boundaries (audit log = changes only; no synthetic rows). The 409→OK path solves a real failure mode (two-tab concurrent discontinue, user-initiated retry after success in another session) with a UX that acknowledges staleness rather than fighting it. The `dropdown-menu` primitive earns its install through known Phase D replication — explicit YAGNI exception. The §6.5 doc-fix for Notes-omission is queued; once landed, the spec stops contradicting reality.

**Date:** 2026-05-29
**Decision:** Phase C item 5 shipped (inline editing + change-log entry creation) with the two-affordance split locked, three forward UX punts captured, and four infra/layout cleanups landed.
**Context:** Item 5 introduces the first surface where users can write to the change-log table from the UI (until now, only Discontinue did). The schema split (4 clinical fields go through change-log, all other fields overwrite via PATCH) was already locked in item 1's PATCH-refuses-clinical rule; item 5 makes that split user-visible via two affordances. Live dogfood of the working surface raised real UX questions the user wanted to discuss before merge: should Edit + Log-a-change be unified? Should typos be recoverable? Should clinical cards be click-to-open? Should Pause/Resume ship in v1? Plus four engineering surfaces surfaced during the session: Supabase pooler saturation, history sort ties on same-day rows, /check D2 layout deviation, and /check E2 empty-row visual bug.
**Choice:** (1) **Two-affordance UX (Edit vs. + Log a change) ratified.** Edit is for descriptive metadata corrections (overwrites in place); + Log a change is for clinical events (writes a `medication_changes` row with reason / changedAt / linkedVisitId). The conceptual line is "is this a real-world clinical event, or is it just fixing metadata?" The unification proposal — make Edit smart enough to write change-log rows when a clinical field is touched — was considered and rejected (see Alternatives). (2) **Typo-recovery via reactive delete is queued for a later release**, not shipped. The hypothesis: most "I typed wrong" recoveries happen after the fact, not at entry time. Reactive delete on individual history rows is cleaner than a "this is a correction" toggle. Constraint when implemented: only the most recent row per field is deletable (older deletes break the oldValue chain). (3) **Clickable clinical cards (Current section → opens log-change dialog with field preselected) queued for a later release.** The user accepted the muted hint below the dose card as sufficient for v1. (4) **Resume from paused → active stays v1.5.** PATCH route comment already locks this; reaffirmed in this session as out-of-scope. Real workflow case noted (parent had a procedure, was paused, now back on) — surfaces in v1.5 alongside the broader status state-machine work. (5) **`db/index.ts` `max: 3` connection cap.** Supabase session pooler (port 5432) is hard-capped at 15 clients. postgres-js's default `max: 10` × Turbopack's multi-worker dev model × stale sessions from prior runs exhausts the cap. Cap each process at 3. Production (Vercel serverless) is short-lived per invocation so 3 is fine there too. Long-term fix is to move DATABASE_URL to the transaction pooler (port 6543); short-term cap is documented inline. (6) **History sort tiebreaker: `desc(changedAt), desc(createdAt)`.** Date-only form input coerces to noon UTC, so multiple rows logged the same day tie on `changedAt`; without a secondary sort, same-day entries land in arbitrary order. `createdAt` (defaultNow()) captures real insert order and tiebreaks deterministically. Applied to both `forPatient` and `forMedication`. (7) **`changedAt` coerced to noon UTC**, not noon-in-patient-tz. For a Pune patient this lands at 5:30 PM IST on the chosen calendar day — still safe from day-boundary flips, no new datetime helper. A precise `noonInTimezone(dateStr, tz)` sibling of `todayInTimezone` is queued for the next time we revisit `lib/datetime.ts`. (8) **D2 fix from /check: Edit + actions row anchored to the H1 row** inside `medication-detail-header.tsx`, not floating above the breadcrumb. The shell provides `setEditing` via `MedicationEditContext`; the header renders the Edit/Done button + `actionsSlot` (passed by the page) on the right side of a flex-justify-between row with the H1 on the left. Discontinued meds collapse both pieces and the header omits the actions cluster entirely (also resolves /check E2 — no empty-row vertical-space bug). (9) **`MedicationDomainError.kind` extended** with three new kinds: `medication_discontinued`, `invalid_status_transition`, `linked_entity_invalid`. Each maps to a specific HTTP code + voice-compliant message + structured `details` payload (per-field `fieldErrors` for the linked-entity case). The discontinue route gains a defensive fall-through for the three new kinds it doesn't raise, preserving the exhaustiveness guard.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **Unify Edit and + Log a change into one affordance** — rejected; the change-log row needs metadata (changedAt / reason / linkedVisitId) that an inline blur cannot capture. Silently writing rows with `reason: null` and `changedAt: now` is the exact failure mode that breaks the demo moment ("the dose was raised on April 3rd" requires the row to know it was April 3rd). decisions.md 2026-05-28 already rejected the auto-append variant; item 5 reaffirmed the conceptual split is correct, not just architecturally convenient. (b) **Preventive correction toggle inside the log-change dialog** ("this is a correction, not a change") — rejected; adds cognitive load to every interaction; most typo discoveries happen after the fact. Reactive delete is the simpler mental model and gets queued instead. (c) **Click-on-clinical-card opens dialog with field preselected** as part of item 5 — rejected for this release; the muted hint below the dose card is sufficient signal; clickable cards is a UX upgrade not a bug fix. Queued. (d) **Ship Pause as a separate `…` menu item alongside Discontinue** — rejected; the dialog's field=Status branch already handles active→paused cleanly and exposes the reason+linkedVisit metadata that a menu-button-then-confirmation flow would not. Pause-via-dialog reads as "this is a meaningful event with context" which matches §1.3's demo moment for status transitions. (e) **noonInTimezone helper added now** to avoid clinical-fidelity gap on backdated entries — rejected for this release; noon UTC is 5:30 PM IST and still on the chosen calendar day in patient view. Helper is queued for the next genuinely awkward datetime case. (f) **Make `db/index.ts` `max` env-driven** (`POSTGRES_MAX_CONNECTIONS`) — deferred; current cap works in both dev (Supabase pooler) and prod (Vercel serverless). Revisit if production throughput needs tuning. (g) **Inline-edit primitive generalized to accept arbitrary `options` props now** — rejected per CLAUDE.md "don't pre-build for hypothetical requirements." The hard-coded `select-form` / `select-category` variants will be generalized when the first Phase D entity (Condition) needs an inline Select. (h) **Sort history rows by `createdAt DESC` only** (drop `changedAt` as primary) — rejected; user-meaningful date is what the rail displays, so the primary sort must match. `createdAt` is purely a tiebreaker. (i) **Defer the test-scenarios doc** until Playwright suite lands — rejected; manual paging + driving-agent use cases benefit immediately, and writing scenarios while the feature is fresh in memory surfaces spec-vs-impl gaps that would be invisible later. Spawned `.claude/commands/test-scenarios.md` to systematize the pattern for Phase D / future items.
**Reasoning:** Item 5 is the load-bearing piece for the §1.3 demo moment — without it, no UI path writes `medication_changes` rows with the `linkedVisitId` + `reason` + `changedAt` precision synthesis needs to say "the dose was raised on April 3rd at the cardiology visit." The two-affordance split looks like UX friction until you remember that clinical events deserve different weight than typo corrections; unifying them would either silently degrade synthesis data quality or force every typo through a heavy dialog. The three UX punts (delete-recovery, clickable cards, resume) are real product surface area but not load-bearing for the demo moment; each has a clean re-entry point when prioritized. The infra cleanups (pool cap, sort tiebreaker, layout anchor, error kind extension) are the kind of "you only catch this by dogfooding" issues that the /check + manual-paging discipline keeps surfacing — five sessions in, the pattern is reliable enough to bake into the test-scenarios skill so Phase D items get the same treatment without remembering.

---

**Date:** 2026-05-29
**Decision:** Phase C item 6 (floating Ask AI button → chat surface) ships as a minimal ephemeral conversation pane, not the full §6.2 three-column shell; the button navigates to a dedicated `/patient/[id]/chat?from=…` route carrying surface context as an entity id resolved server-side; the existing `/api/chat` route gains a UIMessage→ModelMessage bridge.
**Context:** Item 6 wires the previously-inert Ask AI button (rendered on both medication pages with `aria-disabled`) to open chat with the current surface's context pre-loaded (design.md §6.4:1354 + §5.3). Two filesystem facts shaped the scope: (a) `app/patient/[id]/` contains only `medications/` — there is no rail, no dashboard, and the 14-table schema has no chat/conversation/message entity; (b) `/api/chat` already accepts `surfaceContext` and returns `toUIMessageStreamResponse()`, but was only ever exercised by `scripts/check-synthesis.ts` calling `runSynthesis()` directly with ModelMessage-shaped input — the HTTP contract had never been hit by a real `useChat` client (which sends UIMessages with `parts`). Both scoping forks were put to the user in plan mode; both recommended options were chosen.
**Choice:** (1) **Minimal ephemeral pane.** `components/chat/chat-pane.tsx` is a single conversation pane (`useChat` in-memory state, no persistence, no `CHATS` history column, no rail). The full §6.2 three-column shell defers to Phase D, when a rail + dashboard exist and a chat-session store can be designed — building it now would require inventing schema (locked post-Phase A) and a rail that doesn't exist. (2) **Dedicated route + URL `from` param.** The button is a `next/link` to `/patient/[id]/chat?from=medications` (list) or `?from=medication:<id>` (detail). Matches §6.4's "opens the full-screen chat" (a real, refreshable destination) and gives item 7's `View full →` a clean place to land. (3) **Entity id, not slug, in the URL.** Medications have no stored `slug` (slugs are derived at render via `slugify()`/`SlugIndex`), and the button always originates from a page that already holds the id — so id is the robust key. The slug round-trip is item 7's concern (pill → detail navigation). (4) **Surface context resolved server-side.** `lib/chat/surface-context.ts` (`server-only`) turns the `from` param into the human string (`"The user is viewing the medication record for Amlodipine (5 mg, once daily), currently active."`); the raw id never crosses into client code. The chat *page* (server component) resolves it and hands `ChatPane` a finished string, which rides the request body on every turn. (5) **`/api/chat` UIMessage bridge.** Route now `await convertToModelMessages(messages)` before `runSynthesis`; `chatRequestSchema.messages` changed from `{role, content}` to UIMessage shape `{id?, role, parts}`. A malformed `parts` array throws → 400, not 500. `runSynthesis` itself is byte-unchanged. (6) **Starter chips omit "Generate a doctor brief"** — the brief capability is Phase E (§5.7); offering it now would dead-end. (7) **User bubble uses a stone tint** (not the §6.2 terra/clay) pending the brand-accent palette checkpoint (decisions 2026-05-20).
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **Build the full §6.2 three-column surface now** — rejected; no rail exists, no chat-session schema, and schema is locked post-Phase A. Bleeds Phase D shell + Phase E auto-titling/persistence work into C. (b) **In-page overlay/drawer instead of a route** — rejected; isn't really "full-screen chat" per §6.2, and complicates item 7's pill→detail navigation (you'd be navigating out of an overlay). (c) **Slug in the URL (`?from=med:amlodipine`) to match the citation-pill format** — rejected; medications carry no stored slug, so resolution would require a `slugify`-scan over all meds; the button has the id on hand and item 6 is independent of item 7's pill. (d) **Pass the full surface-context string in the URL** — rejected; ugly/leaky URLs and pointless when the server can rebuild it from a stable id. (e) **Keep the route's ModelMessage contract and have the client send ModelMessages** — rejected; fights the `useChat`/`DefaultChatTransport` idiom (UIMessages with parts) that §9.3 prescribes ("Frontend uses useChat"); `convertToModelMessages` is the canonical bridge.
**Reasoning:** Item 6's milestone obligation is the demo loop's middle links — open chat → streamed synthesis response biased toward the entity → (item 7) click pill → land on detail. The minimal pane satisfies every link without over-building the Phase D shell or inventing schema; CLAUDE.md's "easy to over-build" fence (§10.1) and the absence of a chat entity in the 14 tables both point the same way. The dedicated-route choice is the literal reading of §6.4 and is the only option that lets item 7 round-trip. The id-not-slug call sidesteps a fragile slug scan and keeps the slug-resolution problem cleanly inside item 7 where it belongs. The route bridge was always going to be needed the moment a real client hit `/api/chat`; doing it now (with `runSynthesis` untouched) keeps the agent layer stable while making the HTTP contract client-consumable. Pending doc-fixes queued: §6.2 should note that the three-column shell + chat persistence are Phase D, and that a stone-tinted user bubble is the interim treatment until the accent lands.

---

**Date:** 2026-05-30
**Decision:** Reverse the item-6 navigation model — the floating Ask AI button now opens a persistent right-side **drawer** (slide-over) hosted at a new patient-level layout, holding one conversation that survives navigation between entity pages — instead of navigating to a dedicated full-page `/chat` route.
**Context:** The full-page chat route (decided + shipped 2026-05-29) was dogfooded and the user pushed back on two real UX problems: (a) navigating to a separate page hides the medication/wiki content the user is asking about, forcing back-and-forth; (b) because there is no chat-session table (v1 schema, by design), the conversation is in-memory only — so every round trip entity→chat→entity rebuilds an empty thread, actively destroying the conversation the workflow depends on. The drawer alternative had been surfaced (and rejected) during the original plan-mode for "complicating item 7's pill→detail navigation." That objection turned out backwards: with the drawer hoisted to a layout, clicking a pill's `View full →` drives the page *behind* the drawer while the conversation stays open — strictly better than the page version, where pill-navigation would discard the thread.
**Choice:** (1) **Patient-level layout** `app/patient/[id]/layout.tsx` wraps all patient routes in a client `ChatDrawerProvider`. App Router keeps a layout mounted across child-route changes, so the single `useChat` thread persists as the user moves between medications/list/detail. (This layout is also where the Phase D rail will live.) (2) **`ChatDrawerProvider`** (`components/chat/chat-drawer-provider.tsx`) owns the `useChat` instance, the drawer open state, and the conversation UI inside a shadcn `Sheet` (right side). It exposes `useChatDrawer().openChat(surfaceContext)` via context. (3) **`AskAiButton`** becomes a `"use client"` trigger calling `openChat(surfaceContext)` — no navigation; the page stays visible behind the slide-over. (4) **One thread across the patient** (user choice): `surfaceContext` rides each message's request body via `sendMessage(msg, { body: { surfaceContext } })`, so a single conversation is tagged with whichever page the user is on when they send — page-aware framing without resetting the thread. (5) **`surface-context.ts` refactored to pure builders** (`MEDICATIONS_LIST_SURFACE`, `medicationSurfaceContext(med)`) — pages already hold the entity, so no `server-only` DB lookup is needed; the URL-param resolver is gone. (6) **shadcn `sheet` primitive added by hand** on Base UI (`@base-ui/react/dialog`), matching the repo's base-nova style — the shadcn CLI hung on interactive overwrite prompts. (7) **Dropped:** `app/patient/[id]/chat/page.tsx` and `components/chat/chat-pane.tsx` (page-model artifacts). The `/api/chat` route, synthesis agent, `AiMessage`, and citation pills are unchanged.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **Keep the full-page route** — rejected; loses on-screen context and resets the conversation on every round trip, the exact friction the user hit. (b) **Drawer with state in the page (not the layout)** — rejected; would remount and clear on every navigation, defeating the persistence goal. The layout is the only mount point that survives child-route changes. (c) **Fresh thread per entity** — rejected by user; loses cross-entity follow-ups ("how does this compare to the other one?") and re-introduces the reset feel. (d) **Persist the thread to a DB so it survives refresh too** — out of scope; no chat-session entity in the v1 schema (post-Phase A lock), and ephemeral-across-refresh is acceptable for v1. Revisit with the dedicated rail "Chat" surface in Phase D. (e) **Keep the dedicated `/chat` page too, for the future rail** — deferred; the rail "Chat" item is a Phase D §6.2 three-column surface and will be built then, not carried as dead scaffolding now.
**Reasoning:** This is a documented deviation from §6.2 ("full-screen chat") / §6.4:1354 ("opens the full-screen chat"), ratified with the user. The doc's full-screen model keeps the *rail* visible but still replaces the wiki content area — so it doesn't actually keep the *specific entity* in view while asking, which is the whole point of "the thinking partner inside the record." The drawer serves that thesis better, makes item 7's pill→detail loop cleaner, and the layout it introduces is needed for the rail regardless — so it's forward progress, not throwaway. Rework was modest: the route, agent, message rendering, and pills all survived; only the shell (page→drawer) and the context transport (URL param→per-message body) changed. **Pending doc-fixes** (queue for next design.md edit): (i) §6.4:1354 + §6.2 should note the floating Ask AI button opens a context-preserving drawer, with the three-column rail "Chat" surface remaining the Phase D dedicated destination; (ii) note chat persistence (across refresh) is deferred to Phase D alongside a chat-session entity.

---

**Date:** 2026-05-31
**Decision:** Phase C item 7 (citation-pill popover) ships as a medication-scoped slug resolver + interactive popover on `§ med:<slug>` pills only; `View full →` navigates while leaving the drawer open; Phase C is now complete.
**Context:** Phase B left citation pills inert `<span>`s carrying `data-entity-type`/`data-slug` for this item to wire. The core problem surfaced in exploration: medications have **no stored slug** — the chat slug is derived at render via `medicationSlug(m) = med:${slugify(m.name)}`, deduped cross-vault in `vault-context.ts` — while the existing read route is UUID-keyed. So a slug→entity bridge had to be built. Two scoping forks were put to the user; both recommended options chosen. A code-review (`/check`) afterward found one real bug and one misleading comment, both fixed before declaring done.
**Choice:** (1) **Medication-scoped resolver, not a generic citation dispatcher.** `medicationQueries.bySlug(patientId, slug)` matches `slugify(m.name) === slug` over the patient's meds, reusing the *same* `slugify` the serializer used (round-trips by construction); `GET /api/medications/by-slug/[slug]` returns the preview fields + `id` + `patientId`. Phase D generalizes when other detail pages exist. (2) **Resolver returns `patientId`** — the chat drawer is global (can be open over any route), so the pill cannot assume the current route's patientId for the `View full →` href; it comes from the response. (3) **Only `med` pills are interactive.** Non-`med` vault pills and external `↗` pills stay inert, because their detail pages don't exist until Phase D — a popover would dead-end. (4) **`View full →` closes the popover but leaves the drawer open.** The drawer's non-modal push/squeeze + persist-across-nav design (decisions 2026-05-30) makes navigating-with-drawer-open the intended pattern (content reflows beside it), not the overlay-confusion a reviewer assumed. (5) **Fetch on the open *click*** (Base UI Popover is click-triggered), not hover and not on mount — so reading text full of pills triggers no requests; result cached via `requestedRef`. (6) **Palette stays stone-only**; the pill file's old "item 7 picks earth-tone colors" note is superseded by the 2026-05-20 reset and was removed. (7) **`/check` fixes:** error state was permanently bricked (`requestedRef` set before fetch, never reset) — now a transient failure drops the guard so reopening retries, making the "try again" copy truthful; the `bySlug` comment was corrected to state that a `-N` collision-suffixed citation resolves to null → misleading "not in record."
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **Generic `/api/citations/resolve?type=&slug=` dispatcher** — rejected (YAGNI); one branch wired today, builds a dispatch layer for entities that don't exist yet. (b) **Popover on all vault pills with "detail coming soon" for non-med** — rejected; exposes unfinished surfaces and dead-ends the user. (c) **Add a stored `slug` column** — rejected; schema is locked post-Phase A and slugs are a render-time concern, not persisted state. (d) **Close the drawer on `View full →`** (the reviewer's flag) — rejected; premised on an overlay drawer, but this drawer squeezes + persists by design, so navigating with it open is the product intent. (e) **Fetch in a `useEffect` on open** — rejected; tripped `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` and conflated event-response with state-sync. The open handler is the correct home.
**Reasoning:** Item 7 is the final link in the §1.3 demo loop — it makes the citation-discipline promise *clickable* (AI claim → real record), and is the template every Phase D entity copies. Medication-scoping keeps the surface minimal and honest about what's built; the derived-slug match reuses the one source of truth (`slugify`) so emit and resolve can't drift. Known v1.5 limitations, documented in code: collision-suffixed slugs (`med:x-2`) don't resolve (faithful disambiguation deferred), and the preview omits **prescribing doctor** — the demo's cross-doctor field — which lands when the Doctor join/picker arrives in Phase D. Verified: `tsc`+`eslint` clean, smoke 6/6 on edge-case names, resolver curl'd live (5 paths), UI click-through confirmed by user.

**Date:** 2026-05-31
**Decision:** Phase C checkpoint 1 resolved — brand accent locked to **periwinkle** (muted blue-violet, oklch hue 277). A deliberate, recorded deviation from §7.2:2131 "not clinical-blue" / §7.1 "no deep blues / not techy." Ends the palette deferral that has been open since the 2026-05-20 stone-only reset.
**Context:** The 2026-05-20 reset deferred the accent until enough surfaces existed to judge a candidate "in concert rather than in isolation," naming the post-items-3-4 point (now: all of Phase C complete) as the canonical retry. Three prior picks died in the wellness-green register (seafoam → sage → emerald). The user ran a separate Claude Design session and returned with periwinkle. Because periwinkle is blue-family — the *other* explicitly-named §7.2 anti-pattern — it was not applied silently: the conflict was surfaced, then the candidate was rendered live across all five Phase C surfaces (list, detail, form, drawer, pills) as a reversible trial before committing. The user judged it reads "calm, not clinical or techy" on the warm-stone neutrals, and that the warm neutrals did not feel too warm beside it.
**Choice:** (1) **Three source values** from the design session, hue 277: `--accent`/primary 500 `#5b63a0` = `oklch(0.52 0.097 277)`; soft 300 `#a6aeda` = `oklch(0.76 0.064 277)`; tint 50 `#eff1fd` = `oklch(0.96 0.017 277)`; plus 400 `#828bc2`, 600 `#41477c`. (2) **Token mapping in `app/globals.css`** (only the accent-bearing tokens swap; warm-stone neutrals stay): light `--primary` = periwinkle 500, `--primary-foreground` = near-white (passes contrast), `--accent` = periwinkle 50 tint, `--accent-foreground` = periwinkle 600, `--ring` = periwinkle 400. Dark mode derived from the light ramp (inverted lightness, same hue), not yet visually validated — v1 ships light-first. (3) **Accent-only, neutrals unchanged.** The cool-accent-on-warm-paper risk (which would normally force cooling the neutrals) was evaluated live and rejected — the warm-stone reads harmonious, so no neutral rework. (4) **Two hardcoded-stone spots wired to the accent:** citation **vault** pills now use `bg-accent`/`text-accent-foreground` (external `↗` pills stay neutral stone — a vault-vs-external semantic split: inside the record = brand-tinted, outside = neutral); the chat **user-bubble** now uses the periwinkle tint (the §6.2 accent slot, replacing the stone stand-in from decisions 2026-05-29). `View full →` link recolored `text-primary` per §7.2 "no blue for links — use the brand accent." (5) **Unchanged:** `--destructive` stays desaturated red; `--secondary`/`--muted`/`--border`/`--background`/`--foreground`/`--card` stay warm stone; status good/warn/bad semantics untouched. The design session's file instructions (`styles.css`, `.scribble`, `wireframes.html`, `ACCENTS`/`TWEAK_DEFAULTS`) target the standalone HTML wireframe prototype, not the Next.js app — only the oklch values transferred; our tokens are shadcn oklch vars.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **Reject periwinkle as an out-of-bounds blue and send the design session back for an earth-tone** (terracotta/dusty-rose/warm-brown per §7.2:2139) — offered; user declined after seeing periwinkle live read as calm rather than clinical. The §7.2 anti-pattern targets *clinical* blue (saturated, medium-blue, hue ~230-250); muted blue-violet at this lightness/chroma was judged outside that failure mode. (b) **Stay stone-only for v1, defer accent to Phase F polish** — rejected; checkpoint 1 was the designated decision point and all surfaces existed, so deciding now means Phase D builds once against a settled palette rather than re-touching ~13 surfaces later. (c) **Commit periwinkle from the label without rendering it** — rejected; the three failed green picks proved that judging an accent in the abstract is the failure mode. The see-it-live trial (reversible, nothing recorded until approval) is the canonical retry method the 2026-05-20 entry prescribed. (d) **Cool the neutrals to match the cool accent** — held in reserve, not done; the warm pairing was validated live. Revisit only if a later surface makes the warm paper feel disjoint. (e) **Use periwinkle 500 (solid) for citation pills** per the design note's "buttons, pills, send" grouping — rejected for inline citation pills; a solid 500 reads loud sprinkled through body text, so vault pills use the soft tint (`--accent`) and keep "restraint over completeness."
**Reasoning:** This closes a decision that blocked nothing structurally but would have forced rework across every Phase D entity if left until after they shipped. The process matters as much as the pick: blue is a named anti-pattern, so the deviation is *recorded* (here + the §7.2 note in design.md) rather than silent, and it was *validated live* rather than asserted — the same "multi-surface comparison, not single-surface generation" discipline the 2026-05-20 reset called for, just resolved in the user's favor on a candidate the doc's literal text would have excluded. The token swap is centralized (accent-bearing CSS vars only), so the whole system moved with one edit plus two hardcoded-stone cleanups; reverting would be one `git` diff. Dark mode is the one open thread — derived, not validated — flagged in-code for a later light-first-then-dark pass. Pending doc-fix queued: §7.2/§7.1 get a note that the no-clinical-blue / no-deep-blues exclusion is satisfied by muted blue-violet, with periwinkle recorded as the chosen accent.
**/check follow-up (same session):** Three fixes from the post-commit review. (1) **v4 var-syntax bug** — the pill/bubble rings used the Tailwind **v3** bracket form `ring-[--accent-foreground]/15`, which v4 does *not* auto-wrap in `var()` (v4 moved that to `(--var)` parens; the repo's own shadcn primitives use it). The ring color silently fell back to the default; `tsc`/`eslint` can't catch invalid utility strings. Switched to the idiomatic theme utility `ring-accent-foreground/NN`. (2) **Reverted** a gratuitous external-pill text change (stone-800→700, no justification). (3) **Link token** — the `View full →` link first used `text-primary` (periwinkle 500, the accent *base*), but §7.2:2142 says links use "the brand accent in a *darker* shade." Added a dedicated `--link` token = periwinkle 600 (`--color-link` mapped in `@theme inline`; dark = periwinkle 300) and switched the link to `text-link`. Deliberately *not* reused `text-accent-foreground` (same hex today but semantically the on-tint-text token — fragile coupling). This is the reference link pattern Phase D copies, so the token lands now rather than after replication. Re-verified `tsc`+`eslint` clean; no `[--var]` bracket syntax remains repo-wide.

**Date:** 2026-06-02
**Decision:** Phase D — Condition **API layer** shipped (schemas + routes + query helpers), cloning the Phase C Medication template. First Phase D entity, proving the state template generalizes beyond Medication. UI (form, list/detail pages, components) and the `citation-pill.tsx` wiring for `§ condition:` are deliberately out of this slice; the `by-slug` resolver route is built now as the backend half so the pill wiring is unblocked.
**Context:** Schema (`db/schema/condition.ts`) + serializer (`serializers/condition.ts`, emits `§ condition:${slugify(name)}`) already existed from Phase A/B and were correct — no schema changes. Work was filling the Medication-shaped template: `lib/schemas/api/condition.ts`, `app/api/conditions/{route, [id]/route, [id]/changes/route, by-slug/[slug]/route}.ts`, and growing `db/queries/condition.ts` from its two Phase-A stubs to the full helper surface + `ConditionDomainError`.
**Choice:** (1) **Two divergences from the Medication template, both ratified with the user.** (a) `notes` is edited inline via PATCH, **not** change-logged — §6.5 Condition History lists only status/severity/managing-doctor, and Medication treats notes as a plain field. The `condition_changes.field` enum's `"notes"` value is intentionally unused in v1 (no migration); the query helper narrows the loggable field set via `Exclude<…, "notes">` so the change-create switch stays exhaustive over three cases. (b) **All status transitions flow through `/changes`** (`field=status`, `newValue` = any valid status ≠ current) — there is **no `/resolve` route**. Condition's 5-state model (`active↔controlled` is the demo-moment transition) has no terminal state and no resolved-date/reason columns to justify a dedicated route, unlike Medication's `/discontinue`. "Resolve" in the future detail `…` menu is just a status-change POST. (2) **Structural consequence:** `condition_changes` has **no `linked_visit_id` column** (verified against the schema), so `linkedVisitId` is dropped from the change schema/route/helper entirely (Medication's `/changes` carries it). The demo's cross-doctor "raised the dose on April 3rd" is a *medication* change, so condition changes not linking to visits costs the demo nothing. (3) **`ConditionDomainError` kinds pruned** to `not_found | invalid_status_transition | linked_entity_invalid` — Medication's discontinued-specific kinds dropped because there's no terminal block (a resolved condition may recur, so `resolved → active` is permitted). (4) **`linked_entity_invalid` carries a reason *code*** (`doctor_not_found` / `no_op`), mapped to distinct field-error messages in the route ("Doctor not found in this patient's record." / "This doctor is already the managing doctor.") rather than a generic message — actionable client feedback. (5) **Create accepts the change-logged fields directly** (status/severity/managingDoctor) — no prior value to log at create-time, exactly as Medication-create accepts prescribingDoctor; omitted-field semantics are JSDoc'd in the schema (status→active default, severity/managingDoctor/diagnosedBy→null).
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **Dedicated `/resolve` route mirroring `/discontinue`** — rejected; no side-effect columns to write makes it a thin special-case of `/changes`, and the multi-state model wants general transitions, not a terminal route. (b) **`notes` change-logged per the literal Phase 4 enum** — rejected; contradicts §6.5 History and the Medication precedent, and adds a 4th change branch for a field users edit casually. (c) **Block changes on resolved conditions** (mirror med's discontinued block) — rejected; conditions recur, relapse is a real `resolved→active` transition.
**Known limitation (v1, documented here per review):** the no-op status guard rejects `active→active` etc. as `invalid_state_transition` (409). This also blocks a legitimate edge case — a doctor *re-confirming* the current status with a fresh `reason`/`changedAt` as an audit event. Judged acceptable for v1 (rare; user can capture the re-confirmation in notes or a journal entry); faithful "re-affirm current status" audit entries are a v1.5 consideration if the need surfaces.
**Reasoning:** Condition is the Phase D pattern-proof: the Medication template transferred almost verbatim (error helper, runtime decls, JSON-parse guards, validation/flatten patterns, teaching-surface `rejectedFields`, transactional read→validate→log→update, by-slug round-trip via shared `slugify`), and the only friction was where Condition's richer state model genuinely differs — which is exactly the signal worth recording for the remaining state entities (Doctor, Allergy, Lifestyle, FamilyHistory) that copy this. **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint .` clean (exit 0, zero findings); dev server curl battery covered every route — create (name-only→active default + full body), list (+ `?status` filter + bogus-enum 400), get (hit + 404), PATCH (notes/icdCode inline 200 + clinical-field 400 `rejectedFields` + empty-body refine 400), `/changes` (status active→controlled with noon-UTC `changedAt` coercion + parent-row update, status no-op 409, severity 200, managing_doctor happy 200 + `doctor_not_found` 400 + `no_op` 400 + `notes`-field discriminator 400), by-slug (hit preview + miss 404 + invalid-slug 400), delete (204 + re-GET 404 + idempotent-miss 404 + bad-uuid 400). Test data (a seeded doctor + conditions) cleaned from arogya-dev afterward; identity-only seed restored.

**Date:** 2026-06-02
**Decision:** Post-Condition `/check` → all 7 principal-review findings fixed before starting Doctor, by extracting **three shared infra modules** that the remaining ~10 Phase D entities now inherit, and retrofitting the Medication vertical onto them. (Supersedes the prior entry's `ConditionDomainError` kind name — see fix #5.)
**Context:** The Condition API slice was clean against spec, but a principal-engineer review flagged that the Medication-template clone was about to copy several leaf helpers and one inconsistency 10× as Phase D replicates. The judgment call: abstract *now* (2 concrete callers, ~11 known future) rather than after the pattern sets — the textbook non-premature moment. The user directed fixing all findings before moving on.
**Choice:** **New shared modules:** (1) `lib/api/route-helpers.ts` — `coerceChangedAt` (noon-UTC date coercion), `parseJsonBody` (discriminated result), `validateUuidParam` (generic over the param key), `fieldErrorsFromReason` (the unified linked-entity mapper). (2) `db/queries/_shared.ts` — `doctorInScope` / `conditionInScope` / `visitInScope`, patient-scope FK existence checks on the base `db` (existence reads of un-mutated rows; FK constraint is the backstop). (3) `lib/logger.ts` — PHI-aware, `server-only`; the **type signature is the guard rail** (accepts only `{op, code?, ids?}`, no free-form message, no entity param), so `notes`/names/AI output can't leak; `errorCode(err)` extracts constructor-name + string `.code`, never `.message`. **The 7 fixes:** #1 leaf-helper dup → all 9 condition+med routes import the shared helpers; #2 `fieldErrorsFromLinkedEntityMeta` had diverged (med free-text vs condition codes) → unified on reason **codes** (`doctor_not_found`/`no_op`/`condition_not_found`/`visit_not_found`), med retrofitted from English-in-the-error; #3 zero error logging → `logger.error` in every `server_error` catch across **both** verticals (resolves the long-open progress.md blocker per 9.6:2845); #4 `create()` skipped FK scope-check → both creates now validate via `*InScope` → mapped **400** (not Postgres FK-violation **500**) + no cross-patient refs, and the `/changes` inline `tx.select` checks swapped to the same helpers; #5 `ConditionDomainError` kind `invalid_status_transition` → **`status_unchanged`** (it only ever fired on a no-op; Condition has no transition rules — the name carried Medication's semantics wrongly); #6 severity no-op left unguarded → documented in-code as intentional (re-logging harmless, matches Med's free-value fields); #7 `check-conditions-api.ts` could orphan rows on SIGKILL → a `ZZZ-smoke%` pre-sweep self-heals on next run.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) **Defer the abstraction to Phase F polish** — rejected; the whole point of Phase D is replication, so the dup/inconsistency would be baked into 10 more entities first, making the cleanup 10× larger. (b) **Fix Condition only, leave Medication on the old pattern** — rejected; that *is* the divergence (#2), and two conventions for one concept is the tax the review named. (c) **Tx-polymorphic scope helpers** (accept `db | tx`) — rejected as premature; create isn't transactional and the `/changes` scope read is of un-mutated rows, so plain-`db` reads are correct and avoid fragile cross-version Drizzle tx typing. (d) **Runtime PHI scrubbing in the logger** — rejected in favor of a closed type surface; you can't reliably regex-scrub PHI, but you *can* make it impossible to pass.
**Reasoning:** Doing this between Condition and Doctor means every subsequent Phase D entity is a smaller, more correct clone (import helpers, log in catches, scope-check FKs for free) instead of propagating gaps. Medication being retrofitted in the same pass keeps the reference impl honest — it now demonstrates the pattern the others copy, not a stale variant. **Verified:** `tsc`+`eslint` clean (exit 0); `npm run conditions-api:check` 31/31 (twice, idempotent — +2 new create-scope assertions); a Medication curl battery re-confirmed every refactored route (create happy/bad-doctor-400/bad-purpose-400, changes dose + prescribing_doctor-400 code-mapping + status→paused, discontinue 200 + repeat 409); logger verified silent on 4xx, fires only on 500s; arogya-dev left clean of this session's data (4 unrelated Phase-C-era meds left untouched).

**Date:** 2026-06-02
**Decision:** The shadcn `Select` in this project is **Base UI** (`@base-ui/react/select` 1.4.1), not Radix — so `<Select.Value>` renders the *raw value* unless the `<Select>` **root** is passed an `items` map (or array of `{value,label}`). Convention going forward: every `Select` whose option values differ from their display labels MUST pass `items`.
**Context:** The Condition form's Severity/Category triggers displayed the literal sentinel `__unset__`, and Status showed `active` instead of "Active". Root cause traced to `components/ui/select.tsx` wrapping Base UI (not Radix, which auto-maps value→label via the selected item's text).
**Choice:** Pass `items` to the `Select` root on the condition form (status/severity/category) and the condition list filter (prepend the "all"/sentinel option to the shared options array so its label resolves too). The sentinel→undefined submit coercion is unchanged.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) `<Select.Value>{(v)=>label[v]}</Select.Value>` render-function form — works but repeats a lookup at each call site; `items` on the root is the documented idiom and also drives keyboard typeahead. (b) Switch the primitive to Radix — rejected; out of scope, and Base UI is already the project standard.
**Known carryover:** the **Medication** form (form/category selects) and med list filter have the *same* latent bug (trigger shows `tablet`/`all`) — not fixed this session; recorded in progress.md to fix when those surfaces are next touched.
**Reasoning:** This is a cross-cutting trap every remaining Phase D form + filter will hit; documenting the `items` requirement once prevents re-discovering it per entity.

**Date:** 2026-06-02
**Decision:** Condition **form (6.12)** + **list page (6.4)** shaping calls, cloning the Medication UI template — the second proof (after the API layer) that the state template generalizes.
**Context:** Building Phase C items 2-3 for Condition. The form's richer state model and the list card's linked-context line forced several small calls not pinned by the design doc; all confirmed with the user via plan-mode questions.
**Choice:** **Form:** (1) **Severity keeps both** a "Not assessed" sentinel (→ null, "never assessed") *and* the enum literal `unknown` ("assessed, indeterminate") — the API already distinguishes null vs `unknown`, so the UI preserves it. Category uses a "Not set" sentinel → null. (2) **Linked-doctor fields (`diagnosedBy`, `managingDoctor`) and `icdCode` are omitted** from the form — same posture as the Med form deferring prescribingDoctor/purpose; the doctor pickers need the autocomplete + `+ Create new` pattern that lands with the Doctor entity, and `icdCode` is an extraction/API concern not in 6.12. `managingDoctor` is still settable later via `+ Log a change`. (3) **Diagnosed-on has no default** (Med's Started-on defaults to today) — diagnoses are often historical/unknown; empty native picker, no tz plumbing on the page. (4) **Field order** Diagnosed-on **before** Category (the `/check` D1 fix), matching 6.12:1844 with the deferred doctor fields removed. **List:** (5) **Card pills = category (clinical) + status** — the schema has no `primary` column, so the 6.4 sketch's "primary pill" is dropped; both render as neutral tokenized pills (accent still stone-only/deferred). (6) **Linked-counts line built now** — `N medications · N labs monitoring` from `medications.purpose` + `lab_results.linked_condition`, computed by loading both and grouping in-memory (full-patient load, fine for single-patient v1; a `GROUP BY` count query is the scale shape). (7) **Subtitle = non-zero status counts only**, no specialist segment (no doctors linked yet); the subtitle word is derived from `STATUS_OPTIONS` lowercased (the `/check` E1 fix, dropping a hand-maintained dup map). (8) **Managing-doctor renders as inert text**, not a link (Doctor detail doesn't exist — the D2 carryover).
**Alternatives considered:** (a) collapse severity to just `unknown` (drop the sentinel) — rejected; loses the never-assessed vs indeterminate distinction the API supports. (b) defer the card's linked-counts line like the Med card deferred its `↑ recent change` — rejected; it's the cross-entity wiring the north-star demo leans on, and the queries are cheap. (c) extract a shared `<EntityList>`/`Section` now (med + condition lists are near-identical) — deferred to the **Doctor** vertical (rule of three: 3rd copy) and logged as carryover E2.
**Reasoning:** Each call either preserves an API-level distinction (severity null vs unknown), matches an existing Med precedent (deferred linked entities, inert refs), or removes drift (`/check` D1/E1). Recorded so the remaining state entities (Doctor/Allergy/Lifestyle/FamilyHistory) clone the *resolved* shape. **Verified:** `tsc`+`eslint` clean; dev-server round-trip — 4 seeded conditions grouped into the 5 buckets, subtitle `3 active · 1 controlled · 1 resolved · 1 suspected`, `?category=endocrine` filtered to one, bogus filter tolerated, sentinel selections stored as null; test rows cleaned (2 pre-existing conditions `A`/`Knee Pain` left untouched). Commits `1241090` (form) + `f08a98e` (list + D1/E1).

**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Decision:** Condition **detail page (6.5) + inline-edit + +Log a change + Delete + live `§ condition:` pill** — completing the Condition state vertical; plus the root-cause fix for a client-bundle TDZ and two §6.5 linked-context corrections, all from the post-build `/check`.
**Context:** Final slice of the first non-Medication state vertical (proves the §6.5 *detail* template generalizes, after the API/form/list proved the earlier slices). Cloned the Med detail tree; the Condition state model (richer 5-status enum, no terminal state, no `linked_visit_id`) and a `/check` review forced several calls not pinned by the design doc.
**Choice:**
**Detail divergences from the Med template:** (1) **No status-based freezing** — Med hides Edit/+Log-a-change when `discontinued`; Condition has no terminal state (`resolved→active` is valid), so all affordances stay available for every status and the shell drops the discontinued branch. (2) **Neutral tokenized status pill**, NOT the Med header's hardcoded `emerald/amber/stone` (a latent 7.2 violation — left as a med carryover, deliberately not propagated). (3) **`…` menu = Delete only** — FK-safe because `medications.purpose` + `lab_results.linked_condition` are both `onDelete:"set null"` (linked rows survive and null their ref, no 500); Resolve is reachable via +Log-a-change→status; Export deferred (as Med). (4) **+Log a change = status / severity / managing_doctor, no `linkedVisitId`** (condition_changes has no such column); the status select excludes the current value (so the only 409 path is a stale page, not a user no-op); severity offers all 4 (no-ops intentionally permitted, matching Med's free-value fields); the managing-doctor select excludes the current doctor and handles an empty doctor list. (5) **Every `<Select>` passes `items`** (the Base UI label fix) so no Condition surface carries the latent raw-value bug the Med dialog still has.
**TDZ root-cause fix (cross-cutting):** `condition-options.ts` + `medication-options.ts` value-imported `@/db/schema` but used the enums only in `typeof X.enumValues` positions → that runtime edge pulled the whole Drizzle/DB layer into the client bundle and crashed `citation-pill` (loaded early via `chat-drawer-provider`) with `STATUS_OPTIONS is not defined` (an ESM circular-init temporal-dead-zone). Fix: both options files → `import type`. **New convention: entity `*-options.ts` files import schema enums as `import type`** (type-only use; keeps the DB layer out of the client bundle and inoculates the next entity's pill).
**Two §6.5 linked-context fixes (`/check`):** linked meds now render a status pill on the right (§6.5:1382 "status pill where applicable"); the `△` glyph + a flag pill are reserved for genuinely flagged lab markers (`low|high|critical`), not every monitored marker (§6.7 token semantics — avoids overloading `△` before the Lab vertical sets its meaning).
**Alternatives considered:** (a) inline the status labels in citation-pill (the first, shipped band-aid) — superseded by the `import type` root-cause fix, which also kills the label duplication. (b) Freeze affordances by Condition status like Med — rejected; no terminal state. (c) Clone the Med header's colored status pill — rejected (hardcoded-color/7.2 violation; kept neutral tokens). (d) `…` = Delete + Resolve shortcut — user chose Delete-only; Resolve via +Log-a-change. (e) Render all monitored labs with `△` — rejected; overloads the flagged-marker glyph.
**Reasoning:** Each detail call either matches the Condition domain (no terminal state, no visit link), avoids a known latent bug (Select `items`, hardcoded colors), or follows a Med precedent (deferred doctor pickers → inert refs). The TDZ fix treats the cause once so the next entity's pill (Doctor/Allergy) is inoculated. **Verified:** `tsc`+`eslint` clean; live E2E on the resumed arogya-dev DB — render, inline-edit (PATCH + clinical-field rejection + required-blank), +Log-a-change (status active→controlled + 409 re-log + severity), `by-slug` resolve, linked-context (med link + `Active` pill), Delete 204 → GET 404 + linked med's `purpose` set null; all `ZZZ`-throwaway rows cleaned. Lab-flag render path type-checked only (no lab create API yet). **Working tree uncommitted at handoff** — 14 new files + 5 edits.

**Date:** 2026-06-09
**Decision:** Phase D — **Doctor vertical complete** (API · form · list-by-specialty · detail · change log · live `§ doctor:` pill), plus the cross-entity wiring that was queued "for when Doctor lands": shared `<EntityListSections>` (E2), config-driven citation pill (3rd-entity fold), live doctor links + editable `diagnosedBy` on Condition/Med detail (D2), prescriber in the med popover, and the med-header color carryover fix.
**Context:** Third state vertical, cloned off the Condition tree. Phase 4 stores the Doctor schema as free text throughout (no enums, no status machine) and deliberately leaves `doctor_changes.field` unconstrained ("rare changes; often empty section" per §6.5:1401) — so the main calls were which fields are change-logged, how deletion interacts with the one restrict-FK (`visits.doctor_id`), and how far to take the queued cross-entity unlocks.
**Choice:** (1) **Change-logged fields = `specialty` + `clinic`**; name/phone/email/address/firstVisit/notes are plain PATCH (PATCH refuses the two logged fields with per-field guidance). They're the synthesis-relevant transitions ("the cardiologist moved from Apollo to Manipal"); contact details are bookkeeping. Code-level subset only — the DB column is text, no migration. (2) **New `ApiErrorCode: delete_blocked` (409)** for restrict-FK delete refusals — sibling of `invalid_state_transition`, per the convention that domain violations get their own codes. Doctor delete pre-checks visits → mapped 409 with `visitCount` (unreachable until Visit ships a create path; FK is the race backstop). **Patient scope is checked before the visit check** so an out-of-scope id reads 404, never a 409 that leaks another patient's data (a `/check` fix). All other doctor FKs are `set null` — verified live. (3) **`lib/doctor-display.ts`** — `displayDoctorName` (Phase 4 stores "Dr. Sharma" *or* "Priya Sharma"; surfaces hardcoding `Dr ${name}` rendered "Dr Dr. Sharma" — all 8 render sites swept onto the helper), `doctorInitials` (avatar circles; no image upload in v1), `maskPhone` (§6.5:1414 at-rest masking, keep first-4/last-3 digits — reproduces the spec's `+91 98XXX XX140` exactly; raw value visible in the edit input deliberately). (4) **Email omitted from the Add form** (faithful to §6.12:1846) but **email + address get a second detail-grid row** beyond §6.5's four named fields — the schema carries them and this is their only edit surface (recorded deviation). (5) **Specialty grouping is case-insensitive** (free text: "cardiology" ≡ "Cardiology", first-seen casing as label, CSS uppercases headers); filter options derive from the data, not an enum. (6) **E2 extraction:** `components/entity-list-sections.tsx` — sections as data (Doctor's buckets aren't known at compile time), open-state keyed by slug with `defaultOpen` fallback; `medications-list.tsx` + `conditions-list.tsx` deleted, both pages migrated. (7) **Citation pill folded** into one config-driven `VaultEntityCitationPill` (`INTERACTIVE_ENTITY_CONFIGS: med | condition | doctor`) at the 3rd entity per the standing note; med `by-slug` now joins the prescriber so the med popover shows the demo's cross-doctor field. (8) **D2 unlocks:** condition `diagnosedBy` is inline-editable via a new `select-doctor` InlineField variant (PATCH path now scope-checks the uuid in `conditionQueries.update`, mirroring create); managing/diagnosing/prescribing doctor refs on the two detail pages are live links. **List-card doctor refs stay inert text** — cards are whole-card `<Link>`s and nested anchors are invalid HTML; card restructure deferred (recorded deviation from §6.4:1348). (9) **Hygiene from `/check`:** `Object.hasOwn` replaces `key in GUIDANCE` in all three PATCH guards (prototype-chain keys like `"constructor"` were misclassified); med detail header's hardcoded emerald/amber status pill → neutral tokens (closes the 7.2 carryover; repo sweep shows zero clinical color literals). (10) **Infra:** `db/index.ts` now caches the postgres client on `globalThis` + `idle_timeout: 20` — Turbopack HMR re-instantiated the module per recompile and orphaned connections until Supabase's 15-client session-pooler cap blocked everything (`EMAXCONNSESSION`, hit twice mid-session).
**Alternatives considered:** (a) All doctor fields inline-editable, no change log — rejected; History would be permanently empty and `doctor_changes` dead, contradicting Phase 4 specifying the table. (b) Everything change-logged — rejected; routes typo fixes on phone numbers through a clinical-event dialog. (c) Reuse `invalid_state_transition` for delete-blocked — rejected; deletion isn't a state transition, and the memory-logged convention is distinct codes per violation family. (d) Doctor pickers (rich autocomplete + `+ Create new` inline modal, §6.12:1824-25) on the med/condition **Add forms** — **deferred**; the log-change dialogs + diagnosedBy inline select already make every doctor ref settable post-create, and the autocomplete component is its own slice. (e) Per-status colored doctor/med pills — no; neutral tokens, accent stays the one-place periwinkle swap.
**Reasoning:** Doctor is the entity the demo moment names twice ("prescribed by Dr. Sharma", "the cardiologist") — the vertical makes the prescriber navigable everywhere it appears: pill popovers resolve, detail backlinks aggregate the cross-doctor view (meds prescribed / conditions managed / lab orders / visits), and the med popover finally carries the prescriber. The template-generalization signal for the remaining entities: free-text state entities need no enum plumbing (form had zero Selects), and the §6.5 template absorbs per-entity variation (avatar-in-card, derived last-visit, masked phone) without structural change. **Verified:** `tsc` + `eslint` clean; full curl E2E on arogya-dev — create ×3 (incl. honorific name + case-variant specialty), list page (grouping `cardiology · 2`/`Endocrinology · 1`, subtitle, med counts), detail (masked phone, `Mar 15, 2018 · 8 years` duration, backlinks `prescribed`/`manages`, notes), PATCH allowed/refused/bad-email, `/changes` clinic move (server-computed oldValue + parent update + History strikethrough render), by-slug `dr-zzz-rao` round-trip, med by-slug `prescribedBy` join, delete-blocked 409 with a planted visit, delete 204 → 404 + set-null on med/condition refs, prototype-key PATCH falls through to schema strict. All ZZZ rows cleaned; doctors table left empty.

**Date:** 2026-06-10
**Decision:** Form date *defaults* ("Started on", "Changed on") use the **user's browser timezone** (`todayLocal()` in lib/datetime.ts), not the patient's IANA timezone. Deliberate user-directed deviation from design.md 9.6:2803.
**Context:** While testing from the US in the evening, the log-change dialog pre-filled tomorrow's date (already 06-11 in Pune) — confusing to the person filling the form. The 9.6 convention optimized for "a morning session in Pune logging today's event"; in practice the *user* (Avi, US) fills these forms, not the patient.
**Choice:** New `todayLocal()` helper; the three log-change dialogs and the med form compute their own default client-side; the `todayInPatientTz` prop plumbing (pages → shells → dialogs/form) is removed. `todayInTimezone` stays in lib/datetime.ts for future patient-clock semantics. Safe at SSR: dialog content only reaches the DOM client-side, and the med form's date input hydrates client-side.
**Alternatives considered:** (a) keep patient-tz per the doc — rejected by the user as confusing; (b) show both ("today in Pune is…") — over-engineered for a default the user can edit.
**Tradeoff accepted:** a US-evening entry now records the US calendar date even if the event clinically "happened" on Pune's date; the picker still allows backdating. Pending doc-fix: 9.6 date-default note.
**Correction (same-session /check):** the deviation citation is wrong — §9.6's actual date-handling text says "*Display:* convert to user's local timezone", which *supports* this change. The patient-tz default rule actually lives at **§4:243** (Patient.timezone: "All timestamps interpret here") and **§6.12:1842** ("Started on … default today in patient timezone") — those are the lines this deviates from, and the pending doc-fix targets them. Earlier entries citing "9.6:2803" for the patient-tz rule inherited the same mis-cite.
